Bu yüzden ara bir hazırlık adımı eklendi:
- [x] Serbest metinde büyü atma niyeti algılansın (`routes/scene.js`'in `/cast`'indeki aynı mantık: mana kontrolü, D20+hasar ya da iyileştirme) ve gerçek sonuç üretsin.
- [x] Eşya fırlatma (throw) için: x/y hedefsiz bir ortamda ayrı bir "fırlat" mekaniği anlamsızlaşıyor — fırlatılabilir eşyaları "kullan" niyetine dahil etmek (basitleştirme, kabul edilebilir bir kayıp) önerildi, coder'ın onayı bekleniyor. **Karar:** Coder+PM onayladı (cross-session mesajları) — ayrı bir "fırlat" mekaniği eklenmedi, mevcut `resolveConsume` (iksir odaklı "kullan" niyeti) zaten bu ihtiyacı karşılıyor; x/y hedefli fırlatma (boş bir kareye eşya atma gibi) freeform'da kavramsal karşılığı olmadığından bilinçli olarak kapsam dışı bırakıldı. Kod değişikliği gerekmedi.

**Düzeltme (büyü atma):** `actionResolver.js`'e `detectSpellId(text)` eklendi — kısa/genel bir fiil köküyle değil, büyünün kendi (oldukça özgün) Türkçe adının ("Ateş Topu", "İyileştir") metinde geçip geçmediğine bakıyor (kısa-kök false-positive ailesine düşmemesi için bilinçli tercih). `freeformCombat.js`'e `resolveCast()` eklendi — grid'in `/cast` route'uyla AYNI mana kontrolü + D20+hasar/iyileştirme mantığını paylaşıyor; tek kasıtlı fark, Ateş Topu'nun grid'deki "bitişik hücreler" (Manhattan mesafe) AoE'si yerine (x/y yok) mevcut karşılaşmadaki TÜM canlı düşmanlara isabet etmesi (PM onaylı uyarlama). `resolveFreeformAction`'ın öncelik sırasına büyü EN BAŞA eklendi (tespiti en spesifik/kasıtlı sinyal - büyü adının geçmesi; sınıf büyü kullanamıyorsa/manası yetmiyorsa zaten `null` dönüp sıradaki kontrole düşülüyor). `chat.js`'in narrasyon/`actionResult` seçim mantığı `cast` sonucunu (hem saldırı büyüsünün D20'sini hem manasız iyileştirmenin roll'suz halini) kapsayacak şekilde genişletildi.

**Bonus (aynı turda, yaratıcı cron fikir #88):** `PICKUP_PATTERN`'deki `"kaldır"` kökünün de (tester'ın bulduğu "kır"/"al"/"iç" ailesinin AYNISI) "kaldırım" gibi alakasız bir kelimeyle çakıştığı bulundu (`isPickupIntent("Kaldırımda yürüyorum")` → `true` idi) - `"kır"`'daki aynı çözümle (çekimli formlar + kelime-sonu çapalı emir kipi) düzeltildi, `actionResolver.js`'e zaten dokunuyor olmam verimli oldu.

**Test:** `actionResolver.test.js`'e `detectSpellId` testleri (spellId doğru tespit, hiçbiri eşleşmeyince `null`) + fikir #88'in regresyon testi eklendi. `freeformCombat.test.js`'e 5 yeni test — İyileştir'in mana/HP'yi gerçekten değiştirdiği, Ateş Topu'nun aktif düşmana gerçekten hasar verdiği (roll.attribute:"int" ile doğrulanan), Ateş Topu'nun 2 düşmanlı bir karşılaşmada İKİSİNE DE isabet ettiği (grid'in AoE'sinin freeform karşılığı), mana yetersizken sessizce hiçbir mekanik sonuç üretmediği, mana kullanamayan bir sınıfın (fighter) büyü adı geçse bile hiç tetiklenmediği. Backend testleri **289/289** (281 eskiden + 8 yeni: 2 actionResolver + 5 freeformCombat spell + 1 fikir #88), regresyon yok.

**Canlı doğrulama (gerçek çalışan `server.js`):** Gerçek bir Büyücü karakteri oluşturup "Ateş Topu'nu goblin'e fırlatıyorum!" gönderdim — gerçek çıktı: `"Goblin'e Ateş Topu ile 4 hasar verdin."`, `roll.attribute:"int"` (wizard primary), mana `GET /api/character` ile **12→8** (manaCost=4) gerçekten düştüğü doğrulandı. Ardından "Kendime İyileştir büyüsünü uyguluyorum" gönderdim — karakter zaten tam HP'deydi (7/7), mesaj doğru şekilde "HP iyileştirdin" ekini ATLADI (healed=0 dalı), ama mana **8→4** gerçekten düştü (büyünün HP'ye etkisi olmasa bile mana harcandığının kanıtı). Geçici sunucu/dosyalar temizlendi.

#### Tester (bağımsız QA, 2026-08-26, PM tarafından atandı — Faz 12-C'nin "geri dönüşü zor" son kapısı)
- [x] Backend testleri: **289/289** doğrulandı.
- [x] "kaldır" kök fix'i bağımsız doğrulandı: `isPickupIntent("Kaldırımda yürüyorum")` artık `false`, gerçek niyet ("Yerdeki eşyayı kaldırıyorum") hâlâ `true` — canlıda da tekrarlandı, envanter değişmedi.
- [x] Büyü mekaniği canlıda doğrulandı: İyileştir (mana düşüyor, dolu HP'de healed=0 doğru), Ateş Topu tek düşmana doğru hasar veriyor, **2 düşmanlı bir karşılaşmada (İskelet Mezarlığı) AoE gerçekten İKİSİNE DE isabet etti** ("Ateş Topu 2 düşmana çarptı, 1 tanesi yenildi!"), seviye atlama XP'yle birlikte doğru tetiklendi, mana bitince büyü sessizce tetiklenmiyor (negatife düşmüyor).
- [x] **BUG FOUND (HIGH SEVERITY — proposal to block Phase 12-C) → [x] FIXED (coder, 2026-08-26): REAL mechanical results (HP/Mana/XP/Level/Inventory) triggered via `/chat` were NOWHERE visible in the frontend.** The root cause had two parts:
  1. `backend/routes/chat.js:166` — the `POST /chat` response only returns `{ playerMessage, gmMessage }`, it never includes the mutated `character` object.
  2. `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel.tsx` — has no `onCharacterChange`-style prop/callback at all, and in `App.tsx` `<ChatPanel refreshKey={chatRefreshTick} />` takes no other props either. The `character` state is updated ONLY by `TacticalGrid`/`CharacterCard`'s own actions (`onCharacterChange={setCharacter}` etc.).
  **Confirmed definitively with live Playwright:** With a fresh Wizard, I sent "I use the Heal spell" 3 times in a row (never touching the grid, chat-only) — on the server side mana actually dropped **12→0** (confirmed via `GET /api/character`), but neither `HeaderHud` (`❤️ 7/7 🔮 12/12 Lv 1` — NEVER changed) nor, even when I subsequently OPENED the character panel (🎒), did the Mana bar inside it show anything but **12/12** (without a page refresh). So once chat became the default/sole interface with Phase 12-B, a player playing purely through chat (which is now the encouraged flow) gets NO visual feedback whatsoever showing that their HP/Mana/XP/Level have ACTUALLY changed — the data silently stays stale unless the page is manually refreshed or the grid is touched (which Phase 12-C will remove). This effectively invalidates Phase 12-A's core promise ("now a REAL mechanical result") from a user-experience standpoint — the mechanics are real but invisible.
  **Additional (low severity, same root cause):** Even roll-less (no D20) spell casts like Heal show a "Success/Failure" roll badge in chat — because the line `actionResult = freeformResult?.actionResult ?? resolveAction(...)` in `chat.js`, when the cast result doesn't include `actionResult` (the heal branch), falls back to an IRRELEVANT flavor roll from `resolveAction()` as before. Observed live: a guaranteed-success Heal cast showed a "Failure" badge — confusing, but not as serious as the bug itself.
  **Proposal:** (a) Add the current `character` object to the `POST /chat` response (when freeformResult exists) and add an `onCharacterChange` prop to `ChatPanel`, wiring it up in `App.tsx` — the most direct fix. (b) For roll-less casts like heal, leave `actionResult` as `null` and don't show a badge at all (prevent falling back to the flavor roll). This requires a fix PR, I did not change any code.
- [x] Test status: backend **289/289**, frontend **89/89** (unchanged — the finding is at the API contract/prop wiring level, existing tests didn't cover this).

**The spell mechanics and root-fix of Phase 12-C-prep were independently verified, BUT I am NOT APPROVING the transition to Phase 12-C (removing the grid) due to the HUD/character-sync bug above — if the grid is removed before this is fixed, players completely lose any way to see their character's REAL status. Reporting to PM.**

#### Coder fix (2026-08-26, proposal (a)+(b) implemented exactly as suggested)

**Fix (a) — character added to the `/chat` response:** The response of `chat.js`'s `POST /` now always includes the current `character` (whether or not there's a freeform mutation, it may be `null`): `res.status(201).json({ playerMessage, gmMessage, character })`. The return type of `sendChatMessage()` in `api.ts` was updated. An `onCharacterChange?: (character: Character) => void` prop was added to `ChatPanel.tsx`, and `handleSend()` calls it whenever the response's `character` is populated. `<ChatPanel>` in `App.tsx` was wired with `onCharacterChange={setCharacter}`.

**Fix (b) — irrelevant flavor roll removed for roll-less casts:** The `actionResult` selection in `chat.js` was split into three cases: (1) if `freeformResult.actionResult` exists (attack/attack spell), use it; (2) there IS a real mechanical result but no D20 (heal cast/use item/pick up item) → `actionResult=null`, show no roll badge at all; (3) if there's no mechanical result at all (pure roleplay), fall back to `resolveAction()`'s flavor roll as before. This covers the heal case the tester specifically mentioned AS WELL AS consume/pickup (same root cause, extended for consistency).

**Test:** Assertions were added to the existing tests in `backend/tests/freeformCombat.test.js` (attack, heal, use item) verifying that `res.body.character` content is actually up to date AND that `res.body.gmMessage.roll` is `null` for heal/consume; a test was also added for "returns `character:null` when there is no active character". 2 new tests were added to `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel.test.tsx` — verifying that `onCharacterChange` is ACTUALLY called with the character from the response, and NOT called at all when `character:null` comes back (real RTL render + real `userEvent.click()`, not a mocked event). Backend tests **289/289** (assertions added, `it()` count unchanged), frontend tests **92/92** (90 previous + 2 new), `oxlint` clean, `tsc -b && vite build` clean.

**Live verification (real running `server.js`, repeating the tester's EXACT repro):** I created a real Wizard and sent "I cast Heal on myself" 3 times in a row (chat-only, never touching the grid) — in ALL THREE responses, the `character.mana` field came back ACTUALLY UP TO DATE: **12→8→4→0** (previously this field was never present in the response at all). In all three responses, `gmMessage.roll: null` (no more irrelevant "Success/Failure" badge). Finally I compared against `GET /api/character` — mana **0/12**, matches the value from the last chat response EXACTLY (API contract consistent). I note that the frontend side could not be verified in a real browser (no Playwright in this session) — instead, the real RTL render/click tests in `ChatPanel.test.tsx` prove that `onCharacterChange` is called with the correct payload; the backend API contract + frontend prop-wiring test together close out the bug's root cause (both parts). Temporary server/files cleaned up.

- [x] Verified backend **289/289**, frontend **92/92**.
- [x] **Repeated the original repro exactly (Playwright, live browser, chat-only flow, never touching the grid):** With a fresh Wizard, sent "I use the Heal spell" 3 times in a row — HeaderHud **ACTUALLY** updated at every step (🔮 12/12 → 8/12 → 4/12 → 0/12), and when I subsequently opened the character panel the Mana bar also showed **0/12**, matching the `GET /api/character` server-truth exactly. Verified with screenshot.
- [x] Tested each of the three roll-less cast/consume/pickup cases separately to confirm the irrelevant badge was removed: Heal cast, "I pick up the item on the ground" (pickup), and "I drink the Potion" (consume) — **all three show `hasBadge:false`**, no "Success/Failure" badge at all. Also verified inventory stayed in sync (the "Gold Pouch" added by pickup appeared in the panel, and the "Potion" consumed by drinking actually disappeared from the panel).

Not a bug in the application code, just a quirk of my own QA environment, noting it for the record.

### Phase 12-C-prep 2 (found by coder before removing the grid, PM-approved, 2026-08-26)
Before removing the grid, the coder noticed two critical gaps — both verified, approved:
- [x] **No enemy counterattack:** freeform had no enemy-retaliation mechanic at all, the player never took damage, and reaching death/tension/GameOverScreen became impossible. Solution: AFTER every player attack/spell action, if there's a living enemy on the scene, have ONE enemy retaliate using `enemyAI.js`'s same D20+damage+armor-reduction logic (without x/y/movement).
  **Fix:** `resolveEnemyRetaliation(character, state, preferredEnemyId)` was added to `freeformCombat.js` — using `enemyAI.js`'s SAME constants (`ENEMY_ATTACK_MODIFIER=2`, `ENEMY_DAMAGE_DIE=6`) and D20+damage+`getTotalArmorReduction()` logic, hooked to the end of `resolveAttack` and the fireball branch of `resolveCast` (heal/consume/pickup/equip do NOT TRIGGER it — as agreed with the PM, only after an attack/attack spell). `describeEnemyRetaliation()` was added to `chat.js` (hit/miss/death narration, HP status). No special backend handling is needed if `character.hp.current<=0` — thanks to the sync fix, the up-to-date `character` in the response already triggers App.tsx's GameOverScreen logic. **Test:** 5 new tests (real HP drop, NO retaliation once the last enemy dies, miss message, lethal retaliation → `hp.current===0`). **Live verification:** attacked repeatedly on the real server and observed a real HP drop (12→11→1), confirmed HP didn't change on subsequent misses.
- [x] **No equip mechanic:** characters start unarmed by default with nothing equipped (confirmed by existing tests) — without equip, players would stay unarmed (d4 damage) forever. Solution: `resolveEquip` + `isEquipIntent` (conjugation-aware forms for "wear"/"put on"/"don", without repeating idea #88's lesson), same paper-doll logic as the existing `/item/equip`.
  **Fix:** `isEquipIntent`/`EQUIP_PATTERN` were added to `actionResolver.js` (conjugated forms of "kuşanıyor/kuşanırım/kuşanacağım/kuşandım" + word-boundary-anchored imperative "kuşan(?!letter)", and ONLY the conjugated forms of "giy"/"tak" — no bare short stem). `resolveEquip(character, text)` was added to `freeformCombat.js` — it tries a name match (due to Turkish consonant softening, "Kılıç"→"Kılıcı" may not match exactly), otherwise falls back to the first equippable item (same pattern as `resolveAttack`'s target-fallback), same paper-doll logic. **Test:** 5 new tests (real equipping, paper-doll removing the old item, staying silent when there's no equippable item) + 2 tests for `isEquipIntent` (real intent + irrelevant words not triggering it). **Live verification:** sent "I equip the Short Sword" on the real server and confirmed the inventory actually showed `equipped:true`.
- [x] **CharacterCard simplification:** once equip/consume/pickup could all be done via chat, the existing action buttons (Use/Equip/Throw/Drop/Select-spell) became meaningless — reducing CharacterCard to a read-only character sheet (HP/mana/XP/attributes/inventory list, equipped items marked) fits perfectly with the "no menus/buttons" vision. A freeform equivalent for dropping items was NOT ADDED (same reasoning as the previously approved throw decision, low severity/acceptable gap).
  **Fix:** `CharacterCard.tsx` was simplified from scratch — the `castSpell`/`consumeItem`/`dropItem`/`equipItem` API calls, all event handlers (`handleUse`/`handleEquip`/`handleDrop`/`handleThrowClick`/`handleSpellClick`/drag-drop handlers), the `SPELLS` list/spell panel, and all `Props` (`onCharacterChange`/`throwingItemId`/`onStartThrow`/`onCancelThrow`/`castingSpellId`/`onStartCast`/`onCancelCast`/`onChatActivity`/`canUseItem`/`canThrowItem`) were removed — now the only prop is `character`. Paper-doll slots are plain `<div>` instead of `<button onClick>` (unclickable), inventory rows are not `draggable`, no item has an action button (only name/icon/equipped label). The `<CharacterCard>` call in `App.tsx` was updated to pass no prop other than `character`; this change also cleaned up the now-dead-code `hasEnemies`/`playerActionAvailable`/`playerBonusActionAvailable` state and the `handleCharacterChange` function (`tsc` already caught these with a "declared but never used" error). The `throwingItemId`/`castingSpellId` state was DELIBERATELY left in place — `TacticalGrid`'s throw/spell target-selection still needs them, and it will be removed along with the grid in Phase 12-C.
  **Test:** `CharacterCard.test.tsx` was rewritten from scratch (from 329 lines to ~155 lines) — most interactive scenarios were removed (now invalid), read-only render verifications + "no action button/spell panel/draggable AT ALL" regression tests were added. Frontend tests **79/79** (down from 92, due to removed interactive tests — not a regression, a scope reduction), `oxlint` clean, `tsc -b && vite build` clean.
  **Live verification:** started the real dev server with `npx vite` and confirmed a `200` response with no console/compile errors (no browser automation in this session, verified via RTL + real build/dev-server smoke-test — same approach as Phase 12-B).

#### Tester (independent QA, 2026-08-26 — idea #90 + Phase 12-C-prep 2, assigned by PM)
- [x] Verified backend **299/299**.
- [x] Idea #90 (natural-language spell synonyms) code review + isolated test: `detectSpellId` works but I found **two residual false-positives** — `detectSpellId("I'm examining the fireball")` → `"fireball"` (the player is merely EXAMINING an item, not casting) and `detectSpellId("I'm trying to heal myself but I have no materials")` → `"heal"` (the text EXPLICITLY says "no materials", i.e. that they CANNOT do it, yet it mechanically spends mana and heals anyway). LOWER risk than the short-stem family from ideas #88/#89 (because candidate phrases are at least 2 words, less likely to appear by chance) but not entirely zero either — observational sentences like "I examine/see X" in particular can be confused with casting. Low priority, not a blocker.
- [x] Re-verified enemy counterattack live: attacked repeatedly through to an actual death (HP 12→2→0, "you collapse to the ground!" narrated correctly) — mechanic is solid.
- [ ] **BUG FOUND (medium-high severity): `resolveEquip`'s "fall back to the first equippable item if the name doesn't match" logic SILENTLY swaps in a DIFFERENT (usually already-equipped) item when the player tries to equip an item THEY DON'T OWN.** In `freeformCombat.js`'s `resolveEquip`: `equippable.find((i) => lower.includes(i.name...)) ?? equippable[0]` — if there's no name match, it falls back to the FIRST equippable item in the inventory (random/irrelevant), the SAME pattern as `resolveAttack`'s "first enemy if no target specified" fallback, but MUCH more misleading HERE: in an attack, not specifying a target is already an ambiguous intent, but in equip, a SPECIFIC item name is given (`isEquipIntent` only looks for the VERB stem, it does NOT VALIDATE the item name), and if that item does NOT EXIST in the inventory, the system chooses "do something random" instead of "reject the nonexistent request". **Verified live TWICE:** (1) I said "I equip my Shield" (not owned) → the system silently said "you equipped the Short Sword" (because the sword happened to be equippable[0]). (2) MORE SERIOUS: I first actually equipped the Short Sword, then said "I put on my Helmet" (not owned) → the system said "you removed the Short Sword", SILENTLY UNEQUIPPING THE WEAPON THE PLAYER HAD ALREADY EQUIPPED (a combination of toggle logic + wrong target selection). The text at least SAYS which item was affected (not entirely silent), so the player may notice, but the behavior is both misleading and can cause an unwanted weapon loss (especially mid-combat).
  **Proposal:** If there's no name match, do nothing (return `null`, a "you don't have such an item" style narration could be added as GM-side flavor) — unlike attack, I don't think the concept of a "default target" matches user expectations for equip. This requires a fix PR, I did not change any code.
- [x] Independently verified that words like "taktik"/"takas" do not incorrectly trigger `isEquipIntent` (consistent with the coder's test).
- [x] Checked via code review whether a dead character (hp≤0) can still take actions via `/chat`: NONE of `resolveAttack`/`resolveEquip`/`resolveConsume`/`resolvePickup` have a `character.hp.current<=0` guard (only `resolveEnemyRetaliation` checks this internally, not the attack ITSELF). **I tried to verify this live but the result was unreliable** (the shell encoding issue we've had in previous rounds when sending Turkish-character messages via curl/bash recurred, couldn't reach a clear conclusion) — so I'm noting this not as a DEFINITE bug, but as an observation based on code review only. **Practical risk is low:** in the frontend, once `hp.current<=0`, `GameOverScreen` REPLACES the game screen (since Phase 6-C), so a normal user never gets access to the chat input at all — even if there's a guard gap at the API level, the UI already prevents it. Recommend revisiting after Phase 12-C (once the grid's `requireOwnedCharacter()` guard is also gone), but low priority for now.
- [x] Test status: backend **299/299**, I made no code changes.

**Conclusion:** Phase 12-C-prep 2's main goals (enemy retaliation, equip's BASIC function) work, the CharacterCard simplification appears consistent under code review (I could not do live browser QA, neither could the coder — this should be addressed in Phase 12-C's full QA). **I am NOT blocking Phase 12-C (grid removal) on the equip fallback bug** — it's low-medium risk, a self-revealing (not silent) behavior, and since equip will become the ONLY way to do this once the grid is removed, an early fix would be good, but whether the grid stays or goes doesn't change this bug. Reporting to PM, the coder can pick it up when convenient.

### Phase 12-C (once 12-C-prep 1+2 are done) — [x] COMPLETE (2026-08-26, coder's own test/QA)

Verified on the real server that `/api/scene` returns 404, the health check works, and freeform combat (real damage + enemy retaliation) fully works; the backend job on real GitHub Actions (`gh run watch`) (including the real Postgres smoke test) is GREEN.

**Frontend (subsequent commit):** `TacticalGrid.tsx` + its test deleted. All grid wiring removed from `App.tsx`: `showGrid`/`manualGridOpen`/`throwingItemId`/`castingSpellId`/the "⚔️ Map" button, `pendingEncounter`/`continueError`/`continuing`/`handleContinueToNextArea` (the "breather" window and "Continue" button — in freeform, the encounter transition ALREADY happens automatically within the same chat message, no separate step is needed), `handleTurnResolved`/`handleSceneUpdate`, `sceneRefreshTick`/`chatRefreshTick`/`handleChatActivity` (ChatPanel is now fully self-sufficient, it no longer needs an externally triggered refresh). Grid-only functions (`getScene`/`moveToken`/`attackTarget`/`castSpell`/`endTurn`/`consumeItem`/`equipItem`/`dropItem`/`throwItem`) removed from `api.ts`. `Scene`/`SceneToken`/`SceneLoot`/`SpellId` removed from `types.ts`. All grid/paper-doll-interaction/spell-list/inventory-actions CSS removed from `App.css` (CSS bundle **17.56kB→11.99kB**, JS bundle **221kB→209kB**). **Accepted minor loss (PM-approved):** the "you cleared X encounters" summary on the Game Over screen is no longer shown — it was sourced from the grid's `scene.encounterIndex`, and freeform does not yet expose this information via an endpoint; since the `GameOverScreen` prop is optional, it renders fine with `null`, and this can be brought back later with a small endpoint.

There was no browser automation tool in this session, so a literal visual verification could not be done — noting this in the interest of honesty.

119. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-2a) (Defensive hardening, low probability/high impact — found by PM, PM role taken on this round, verified via code review) The `JSON.parse(row.data)` calls in `loadAll()` are not inside a try/catch — a SINGLE row in the DB containing corrupted/invalid JSON prevents the ENTIRE server from starting.** I read `loadAll()` in `persistence.js` (lines 90-104): for `characters`/`chatHistories`, `JSON.parse(row.data)` is called on EVERY row returned from `db.getAllCharacters()`/`getAllChatHistories()`, with no try/catch at all. `server.js`'s `start()` function awaits this via `await loadAll()`, and on error `start().catch((err) => { console.error(...); process.exit(1); })` fires — meaning a SINGLE `JSON.parse` error inside `loadAll()` (confirmed with `node -e` that `JSON.parse` throws on corrupted input) causes `app.listen()` to never be called, crashing the server entirely on startup. **Practical probability is low but not zero:** in the normal flow, `saveCharacter`/`saveChatHistory` always write via `JSON.stringify(...)`, and `upsertCharacter`/`upsertChatHistory` is a single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` query (guaranteed by the SQL engine that a half-written/corrupted string can never be persisted) — so the codebase ITSELF does not create this risk. But if a manual DB intervention (e.g. a migration script, manual data correction, a disk/replication issue) corrupts a SINGLE row, the result is that ALL users lose access (a new single point of failure, a DoS-like impact) — instead of the normally expected "skip that row, load the rest, log the problem" behavior. **Proposal:** Add separate try/catch blocks to the two loops in `loadAll()` — when a corrupted row is found, log it with `console.error` (naming which `id`/`session_id`) and SKIP that row, letting the loop/server startup CONTINUE. A small, clear defensive layer (consistent with idea #56's philosophy of "don't silently swallow unexpected errors, log them but keep processing") — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** separate try/catch blocks were added to both the `characters`/`chatHistories` loops in `loadAll()` — when a corrupted row is found, it's logged with `console.error` including the relevant `id`/`session_id` and that row is skipped, while the loop/server startup continues. The `activeCharacterIdBySession` loop was left untouched (it never calls `JSON.parse`, it uses the string directly). **Test:** 2 new tests added to `persistence.test.js` — inserting a `characters`/`chat_histories` row containing corrupted JSON directly via raw SQL, confirming `loadAll()` does NOT throw, the corrupted row is skipped, and the healthy row RIGHT NEXT TO IT in the same call still loads correctly. Backend tests **216/216** (from 214 to 216, no regression). **PM note:** I read the code — try/catch was correctly applied in both loops, the affected id/session_id is logged, and the `activeCharacterIdBySession` loop (which never calls JSON.parse) was correctly left untouched. I also independently confirmed `npx vitest run` (216/216) myself. Push approved, pushed. Full independent tester QA is still pending. **Independent tester verification (claude-game-ff):** I read both try/catch blocks in `loadAll()` — the corrupted row is logged and skipped, the loop continues, and the third loop (which doesn't call JSON.parse) was correctly left untouched. I read the two new tests — they correctly cover mixing a corrupted+healthy row in the same call and confirm the healthy one still loads. Ran independently: backend 216/216, frontend 70/70. Approved, found no additional issues.

118. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-2a) 🚨 IMPORTANT — DATA LOSS RISK (a continuation of idea #117's discovery method, found by PM — PM role taken on this round, verified via code review) The `updated_at` field on the `sessions` table (the ONLY field idea #1's "stale session" cleanup relies on) is only updated at the moment a character is CREATED — no subsequent gameplay activity like chat/attack/spell refreshes it. Result: an ACTIVELY-playing user's character/session for 30 days can be wrongly counted as "stale" and DELETED.** I found all call sites with `grep -rn "saveActiveCharacterId|upsertSession" backend/routes/*.js backend/services/persistence.js`: `saveActiveCharacterId`/`upsertSession` (i.e. the ONE function that updates `sessions.updated_at` via `Date.now()`) is called ONLY in `character.js:124` — i.e. ONLY in the `/character/create` route. I read all of `chat.js`: it calls `saveChatHistory`/`saveCharacter` (updating message/character data) but never calls `saveActiveCharacterId`. The `chat_histories` table itself also has no timestamp column at all (I read `upsertChatHistoryStmt` — only `session_id`/`data`). The `getStaleSessions()` query used by `cleanupStaleSessions()` looks ONLY at `sessions.updated_at`. **Concrete scenario:** A player creates a character on 2026-01-01 (`updated_at` = that moment), and even if they play actively via chat EVERY DAY for the next 40 days (since `saveChatHistory`/`saveCharacter` calls never touch `updated_at`), `updated_at` stays frozen — on 2026-01-31 (the 30-day threshold), `cleanupStaleSessions()` permanently deletes this ACTIVE player's character/chat history/session via `clearSession()`, and the player sees their character become "no active character" (404) the next time they send a message. This is the EXACT OPPOSITE behavior of idea #1's intent — instead of cleaning up sessions that "never come back", it punishes users who have been PLAYING for a long time but only created a character once. **Proposal:** `chat.js` needs to call `saveActiveCharacterId(sessionId, character.id)` at the end of every successful request (or at least periodically, not on every single request — for performance), or a separate, lightweight `touchSession(sessionId)` function should be added to `db.js` to update `updated_at` — chat needs to be the real "last activity" signal, not just character creation. Not a big change (a few lines + one export to persistence.js) but a behavior-affecting fix that needs careful testing — assigned to the coder; doesn't carry architectural risk so doesn't need a big decision, but I'm flagging it as "🚨 IMPORTANT" in TASKS.md because it can cause active user data loss. **Fix:** the proposed `touchSession(sessionId)` was added to `persistence.js` — it reads the EXISTING mapping in `activeCharacterIdBySession` and calls `db.upsertSession()` again with that exact same value (the same `ON CONFLICT` pattern that updates `updated_at`), never accidentally dropping characterId to `null`. It's called in `chat.js`'s `POST /` as soon as `sessionId` is computed (BEFORE the first early return, so ALL successful paths are covered, including the dead-character check). Performance note: this adds one extra `upsertSession` write per chat message, but since it's called with the `fireAndForget` ("fire and forget") pattern, it doesn't block the request response time — the SAME risk profile as the existing `saveChatHistory`/`saveCharacter` calls. **Test:** 3 new tests added to `persistence.test.js` — confirming that `touchSession()` refreshes `updated_at` while preserving `active_character_id`, that it writes `NULL` without crashing for a session with no character yet, and that an end-to-end `/chat` request GENUINELY confirms `cleanupStaleSessions()` no longer DELETES a session that was created 31 days ago but was just active in chat (idea #118's exact scenario). Backend tests **214/214** (from 211 to 214, no regression). **PM note (extra rigorous verification given the data loss risk):** I read `touchSession()` — it reads the in-memory `activeCharacterIdBySession` and calls `upsertSession` with the same value, correct. I confirmed that in `chat.js:149`, it's called as soon as `sessionId` is computed (BEFORE the dead-character early-return) — all successful/failed chat requests are covered. I stood up a real server and confirmed character creation+chat flow works fine (no regression). I read the third test in `persistence.test.js` (lines 209-239) — it verifies end-to-end via a real `/character/create` + `/chat` flow, backdating `updated_at` and confirming `cleanupStaleSessions()` no longer deletes it, covering the exact original scenario. I also independently confirmed `npx vitest run` (214/214) myself. Push approved, pushed. Since this closes a data loss risk, I will flag it specifically for the tester. **Independent tester verification (claude-game-ff, I looked especially carefully due to the data loss risk):** I read `touchSession()`, confirmed it reads the in-memory `activeCharacterIdBySession` and calls `upsertSession` with the current value (no risk of accidentally dropping characterId to null). I confirmed in `data/dbSqlite.js`/`data/dbPostgres.js` that `upsertSession` correctly refreshes via `updated_at = excluded.updated_at` on both the SQLite and Postgres side. I confirmed the call site at `chat.js:149` is as soon as `sessionId` is computed, BEFORE the dead-character early-return. I read the third test (lines 209-243, end-to-end: create character → backdate → chat → cleanup → not deleted), it covers the exact original scenario, and the other two tests correctly cover edge cases (characterId preservation, session with no character yet). Ran independently: backend 214/214, frontend 70/70 (saw 9 transient frontend failures from system load on the first run, all passed on the second run — unrelated to backend/#118, environment-caused). Approved, found no additional issues — this critical data-loss-risk fix is correct and safe.

117. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-2a) (a piece left incomplete from idea #1, found by PM — PM role taken on this round, verified via code review) `cleanupStaleSessions()` (idea #1, the maintenance job that cleans up sessions inactive for 30 days) never touches the `freeformEncounters` in-memory Map — stale sessions' freeform encounter state accumulates in RAM forever.** I read `cleanupStaleSessions()` in `backend/services/persistence.js`: it calls `clearSession()` (deletes character/session/chatHistory from the DB) and clears the `activeCharacterIdBySession`/`chatHistories` in-memory Maps via `.delete(sessionId)` — but the `freeformEncounters` Map (`services/freeformEncounter.js`) is never mentioned. `grep -n "resetFreeformEncounter" backend/services/persistence.js backend/server.js` returned zero results — this function is called ONLY in `character.js`'s `/reset` route (when the user manually says "restart"), not in the automatic/periodic cleanup. I also read `clearSession()` itself (lines 41-48) — it doesn't touch it either. **Impact:** `freeformEncounters` is already deliberately NOT persisted to the DB (a Phase 12-A decision, RAM only) — meaning it naturally resets every time the server restarts (Render deploy/restart), so there's NO permanent disk-growth risk. But if Render's free-tier services can run for a long time (weeks) without restarting, the encounter state (a small object: `encounterIndex`/`enemies`/`loot`) of EVERY session that hasn't come back for 30+ days keeps accumulating in RAM — not large data like `characters`/`chatHistories`, but still an overlooked gap inconsistent with idea #1's intent that "EVERYTHING belonging to a stale session gets cleaned up". **Proposal:** add a `resetFreeformEncounter(row.session_id)` call to `cleanupStaleSessions()` in `persistence.js` (inside the loop, at the same spot as `chatHistories.delete`) — it will need to import `freeformEncounter.js` already. A small/clear task — assigned to the coder (along with the existing #116, the coder can handle both when they return). **Fix:** `resetFreeformEncounter` was imported into `persistence.js` (no circular dependency risk in `freeformEncounter.js` — checked, that file only imports `nanoid`/`data/encounters`), and `resetFreeformEncounter(row.session_id)` was added to `cleanupStaleSessions()`'s loop at the SAME spot as `chatHistories.delete`. **Test:** A new describe block was added (`persistence.test.js`) — using a real in-memory SQLite, a session's freeform state is advanced and the DB's `updated_at` is backdated to 31 days ago, and after `cleanupStaleSessions()`, `getFreeformEncounter()` is confirmed to return RESET (encounterIndex 0); a second test confirms a fresh (non-stale) session is unaffected. Backend tests **211/211** (including the tests added by idea #116, no regression). **Independent tester verification (claude-game-ff):** I personally confirmed the `resetFreeformEncounter` call in `persistence.js` and that there's no circular dependency in `freeformEncounter.js`. I read the two new tests in `persistence.test.js` (stale→reset, fresh→unaffected), they correctly cover the behavior with real in-memory SQLite. Ran independently: backend 211/211, frontend 70/70. Approved, found no additional issues.

116. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-28) (a close-relative gap to idea #115, found by PM — PM role taken on this round, verified via live `node -e`/fetch) The `413 "entity.too.large"` error thrown by `express.json()` when a body that's too large (>100kb default limit) is sent is NOT covered by idea #115's general error middleware in `server.js` — it still falls through to `500`.** I tested this in isolation with `node -e`: when a JSON body over 100kb is sent, `express.json()` genuinely throws `err.status=413`, `err.type="entity.too.large"` (correct behavior). But the check at `server.js:111` covers ONLY the `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"` case (corrupted JSON, idea #115) — since `entity.too.large` doesn't match this `if`, it falls through to the `else` branch, gets logged with `console.error`, and returns `500 "Server error."`. I confirmed this directly by sending a `POST /character/create` with a `name` field of 200KB to a live server (`fetch` via `node -e`): `status: 500`, `{"error":"Server error."}` — instead of the expected `413`/`400`. **Impact:** the exact same class of issue as idea #115 (a client error reported as a server error, mixed in the logs with real server crashes) — just a different trigger (an oversized body instead of corrupted JSON). Practical risk is low (the frontend already sends requests constrained by `MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH=500`/`MAX_NAME_LENGTH=50`, this only triggers if a large body is sent directly to the API) but it's still an incompletely-closed part of the same root cause. **Proposal:** broaden the check in `server.js` — instead of (or in addition to) `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"`, a general check like `err.status && err.status >= 400 && err.status < 500` could be used ("this is already a client error, trust the status express/body-parser itself supplied") — extending the pattern in idea #115's test file is enough. A small/clear task, can be given to the coder who fixed idea #115 for the familiarity advantage — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** the check at `server.js:113` was generalized to `err.status && err.status >= 400 && err.status < 500` (instead of `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"`) — both corrupted-JSON (400) and oversized-body (413) now return with the correct status code. A 413 scenario was added to `errorMiddleware.test.js`. Confirmed live via `node -e`/fetch that both corrupted JSON and oversized body return the correct code. **Commit history note (for honesty, per AGENTS.md's shared-working-directory rule):** this code change actually went out in commit `ee54cfb` ("test: idea #106 ... approved by tester") — while the tester (`claude-game-ff`) ran `git add` for TASKS.md, coder-28's `server.js`/`errorMiddleware.test.js` changes, already staged in the same working directory, were accidentally included in the same commit as well. The code is correct and the tests pass (independently verified), only the commit message never mentions this change — history was not rewritten, it's explicitly documented here. **PM note:** I read `server.js` and independently confirmed the code is genuinely correct, and that `npx vitest run` gives 211/211. **Independent tester verification (claude-game-ff):** I read the generalized check at `server.js:113` — I confirmed (via grep) there's no other code in the routes that sets `err.status`, meaning this generalization only catches body-parser errors, with no risk of masking another error class. I read the 413 test (`errorMiddleware.test.js`), it correctly covers the scenario. Ran independently: backend 211/211, frontend 70/70. I also saw the commit mixup note and confirm it — the code is correct and consistent with my independent run. Approved, found no additional issues.

115. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-a8) (Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round, verified via live curl + node) A client sending corrupted/invalid JSON gets `500 Server error` instead of `400 Bad Request` — the general error middleware in `server.js` (idea #56) doesn't distinguish `express.json()`'s own body-parse error (which already carries the correct status code).** I stood up a live server and sent a corrupted body (`{"name": "broken json`) with `Content-Type: application/json` to `POST /character/create` — it returned `{"error":"Server error."}` (HTTP 500). I tested in isolation with `node -e` what error `express.json()` actually throws: `err.status`/`err.statusCode` is already `400`, `err.type` is already `"entity.parse.failed"` — meaning express itself supplies the correct information, but the general middleware at `server.js:105-108` (`app.use((err, _req, res, _next) => { ... res.status(500).json(...) })`) converts EVERY error to 500 without checking this. **Impact:** (a) semantically wrong — a client error (a malformed request body) is reported as a server error; (b) misleading for monitoring/logs — logs of the client's badly-formed requests get mixed in with real unexpected server crashes (idea #56's actual purpose), making it harder to distinguish a genuine issue; (c) practical risk is low (the frontend always sends valid JSON already, using `JSON.stringify`) — this ONLY triggers when a corrupted request is sent directly to the API (curl/script/malicious client). **Proposal:** add a check to `server.js`'s general error middleware, before falling through to 500, for `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"` (or more generally, if `err.status`/`err.statusCode` is in the `4xx` range) — for such an error, return a message like `{error: "Invalid request body."}` with `err.status` (or 400 default), and don't log it with `console.error` (not a real server error). A small/clear task — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** an `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"` check was added to the final general error middleware in `server.js` — in this case, 400 is returned with `{error: "Invalid request body."}` WITHOUT running `console.error`, while ALL other errors still fall through to 500 as before. **Live verification:** stood up the server for real and re-ran the original curl repro — it now returns 400, no more 500. **Test:** A new `errorMiddleware.test.js` was added (since `server.js` itself unconditionally runs `app.listen()` even under VITEST, an exact copy of the middleware logic using the SAME pattern as other test files is verified in a small test app) — 2 tests confirming corrupted JSON gets 400 and valid JSON is still processed normally (no regression). Backend tests **208/208**. **PM note:** I read the code line (the `err.type === "entity.parse.failed"` check), exactly the fix I proposed. I ran `npx vitest run` a few times — the system was under heavy load at the time (parallel sessions), I saw flaky failures of 1 and 3 tests in a row, but running `errorMiddleware.test.js` in isolation gave a clean 2/2, and once the system calmed down the full suite gave a clean 208/208 — consistent with the coder's finding that "server.js runs app.listen() even under VITEST, can't be required directly", a test-infrastructure constraint, not a code issue. Push approved, pushed. Full independent tester QA is still pending.

114. **[2026-08-31] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-a8) ⚠️ SECURITY (Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round, verified via live curl) The `RACES[raceId]`/`CLASSES[classId]` validation doesn't use `Object.hasOwn` — if `raceId`/`classId` is sent as the name of one of JS's built-in `Object.prototype` members like `"constructor"`, the `!race`/`!cls` check is WRONGLY passed, and character creation subsequently crashes with a 500 error.** I confirmed directly with `node -e`: `RACES['constructor']` → `[Function: Object]` (truthy!), `CLASSES['constructor']` → likewise truthy but `.baseHp`/`.startingInventory` are `undefined`. I stood up a live server and sent `{"raceId":"constructor","classId":"constructor"}` to `POST /character/create` — **it returned `{"error":"Server error."}` (500)**, instead of the clean `400 "Invalid race/class: ..."` message the route normally gives for an invalid race/class. Root cause: since `RACES`/`CLASSES` are plain `{}` object literals, keys they inherit from `Object.prototype` like `constructor`/`toString`/`hasOwnProperty`/`valueOf` return "found" (truthy) when queried via `RACES[key]`, but they are NOT real race/class data — subsequent code (`race.attributeBonuses`, `cls.baseHp`, `cls.id`, etc.) accessing `undefined` fields leads to the 500. **Impact:** (a) on an unauthenticated-accessible endpoint (rate-limited but not blocked), every such request writes an "Unexpected server error" exception to the server logs — noise/log pollution; (b) in `/roll-stats` (`raceId="constructor"`) there's NO crash, but it silently returns a bonus-less result (`applyBonuses(rolls, undefined)` is safe because it uses `bonuses || {}`) — less risky, but it should give an "invalid race" error and doesn't. There's no classic prototype-pollution attack chain here (object mutation; this is read-only), but the pattern of "indexing an object directly with user input, without a hasOwnProperty check" still conflicts with idea #29/#63's principle of "never trust client input." **Proposal:** wrap all the `RACES[raceId]`/`CLASSES[classId]`/`APPEARANCES[appearanceId]` lookups with `Object.hasOwn(RACES, raceId) ? RACES[raceId] : undefined` (or `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call`) — the same pattern at the three sites in `character.js` (lines 57-58, 77-78, 85). Alternative: define `RACES`/`CLASSES`/`APPEARANCES` with `Object.create(null)` (cuts the prototype chain entirely, a root-cause fix) — but this changes how `data/dnd.js`/`data/appearances.js` define themselves, more invasive. A small/clear security fix (the hasOwn wrap is preferred, less risky) — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** the proposed `Object.hasOwn` wrap pattern was applied to all THREE sites in `character.js` — the `RACES[raceId]` in `roll-stats`, and `RACES[raceId]`/`CLASSES[classId]`/`APPEARANCES[appearanceId]` in `create`. `APPEARANCES[character.appearance]` (line ~139, `/intro`) was left OUT of scope — that value is ALWAYS an `appearance?.id ?? null` the server itself produces in its own `/create` (not user input), so it doesn't carry the same attack surface. **Test:** 4 new regression tests added to `character.test.js` (confirming that sending `constructor`/`hasOwnProperty`/`toString` as `raceId`/`classId`/`appearanceId` returns a clean 400 instead of 500, for both `/create` and `/roll-stats`). Backend tests **206/206** (from 202 to 206, no regression — in one round an unrelated test in `db.test.js` gave a flaky timeout from parallel-session load, passed cleanly alone and on a second run of the full suite, not a genuine regression). **PM note (extra rigorous verification since this is a security matter):** I confirmed via `grep` that the `Object.hasOwn` protection was correctly applied at all 4 sites (roll-stats + the three in create), and confirmed by reading the code the rationale for the untouched lookup in `/intro` (not user input, the server's own generated `appearance?.id`) — a sound scoping decision. I stood up a live server and re-ran the original repro (`raceId="constructor"`, `raceId="toString"`) — both now return a clean `400 "Invalid race: ..."`, no 500. I also separately confirmed a legitimate character-creation request still works fine (no regression). I ran `npx vitest run` twice — on the first run I also saw the flaky db.test.js failure the coder mentioned, the second run gave 206/206 — I confirm the coder's diagnosis of "environment-caused, not a real regression." Push approved, pushed. Full independent tester QA is still pending — since this is a security matter I will flag it specifically for the tester.

113. **[2026-08-31] [x] PARTIALLY FIXED (coder, claude-game-a8) (minor code cleanup, proven — Creative cron, found by PM, deep scan: found by cross-checking every `module.exports` individually) Three `module.exports` symbols (`SLOT_ICONS`, `MOCK_OUTCOME_SUFFIX`, `MAX_CHAT_HISTORY`) are not imported anywhere (including test files) — completely unused exports.** I listed ALL `module.exports` lines in `backend/data/*.js`/`backend/services/*.js` and checked each symbol individually with `grep -rln`: others like `WEAPON_DAMAGE_DIE`/`UNKNOWN_WEAPON_DAMAGE_DIE`/`MODEL_NAME`/`getStatus` are at least used in their own test files (legitimate), but `SLOT_ICONS` in `itemSlots.js` (the function `getIconForSlot()` uses it internally but the symbol itself is imported nowhere), `MOCK_OUTCOME_SUFFIX` in `narrationService.js`, and `MAX_CHAT_HISTORY` in `chatHistoryLimit.js` — all three only appear in the file where they're defined, referenced NOWHERE else (application code or test). **Not a definite bug** — just an unnecessary public API surface (not dead code, the functions themselves are still used, it's just pointless that these three constants are exported). **Proposal:** remove all three from `module.exports` (they can stay as `const`/private inside the file, just not exposed) — `itemSlots.js`: `module.exports = { SLOTS, ITEM_SLOTS, getSlotForItem, getIconForSlot }`; `narrationService.js`: `module.exports = { generateNarration }`; `chatHistoryLimit.js`: `module.exports = { trimChatHistory }`. Before making the change, re-confirm in each file that these symbols are genuinely used only within their own file (that removing them from the export list breaks no `require(...).SYMBOL`). 111. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-a8) (a separate finding the coder noticed in #110, independently verified and formalized by PM) Some roots in `gmFlavor.js`'s `LOOK_PATTERN` (and probably other categories) fail to catch their own CONJUGATED forms due to Turkish vowel elision — a false-NEGATIVE (the opposite of idea #110's false positives).** I confirmed directly with `node -e`: `generateGmResponse("I'm examining the room")` → returns a sentence from `GENERIC_RESPONSES`, NOT from `LOOK_RESPONSES` — because the `"incele"` (examine) root in `LOOK_PATTERN`, in the present-tense conjugation, undergoes vowel elision to become "inceliyorum" (the string "incele" doesn't literally appear INSIDE the word, it becomes "incel"+"iyorum") and the regex fails to match it. The SAME root cause as `actionResolver.js`'s `PICKUP_PATTERN`'s "topla"(collect)→"topluyor" issue (the same vowel-elision problem, which was fixed by separately adding "toplu"). Other bare roots like `gözlemle`(observe)/`ilerle`(advance)/`keşfet`(explore) may carry the same risk (not yet tested individually). **Impact:** when a player naturally types "inceliyorum" (I'm examining) (when falling back to the AI mock), they see an atmospherically IRRELEVANT GENERIC sentence instead of a topic-appropriate LOOK sentence — not a definite bug (a sentence is still returned, just the wrong category) but a consistency gap that's the exact opposite of the problem idea #110 solved. **Proposal:** following `actionResolver.js`'s "topla"/"toplu" pattern, separately add the conjugated form (e.g. "inceliyor", "gözlemliyor") for every bare root carrying vowel-elision risk (incele, gözlemle, ilerle, keşfet, etc.) — should be tested and confirmed individually with `node -e`. A small/clear task — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** `"inceliyor"`/`"gözlemliyor"` were added to `LOOK_PATTERN`, and `"ilerliyor"`/`"keşfediyor"` (the vowel-elided conjugated forms) were added to `MOVE_PATTERN`, using the EXACT SAME approach as the `topla`/`toplu` pattern — the bare roots weren't removed (forms that appear separately at the start of a word are still caught), only the conjugated form was added in addition. The `araştır`(investigate)/`yaklaş`(approach)/`koş`(run)/`büyü`(cast)/`sihir`(magic)/`efsun`(spell) roots were tested individually with `node -e` — all of them already correctly catch their own conjugated forms (they don't carry vowel-elision risk, no problem), left unchanged. **Test:** 4 new tests added to `gmFlavor.test.js` — confirming "inceliyorum"/"gözlemliyorum"/"ilerliyorum"/"keşfediyorum" now correctly trigger the right category (LOOK/MOVE). Backend tests **201/201** (from 197 to 201, no regression — idea #110's false-positive tests still pass). **PM note:** I re-ran the original repro (`generateGmResponse("I'm examining the room")`) with `node -e` — it now correctly falls into the LOOK category. I also independently confirmed `npx vitest run` (201/201) myself. Push approved, pushed. The coder's decision to test the other bare roots (araştır/yaklaş/koş/büyü/sihir/efsun) individually and leave them alone was also sound — no unnecessary changes were made.

109. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder, claude-game-a8) (Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round) The AI GM prompt includes the current player message TWICE — `recentMessages` already carries the message, and `buildPrompt` ADDITIONALLY appends it as a "Player: ..." line.** `chat.js:152` calls `history.push(playerMessage)`, THEN `chat.js:200` computes `recentMessages: history.slice(-HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE)` — meaning `recentMessages`'s LAST element is already the current player message itself. The same `generateNarration` call ALSO separately passes `playerMessage: playerMessage.text` as its own parameter (`chat.js:201`), and `buildPrompt()` in `aiGm.js` appends it BELOW `historyText` as "Player: ${playerMessage}" (line 103). Result: in the prompt sent to the AI, the current message appears TWICE — both as the LAST line of the "Recent conversation:" section and as the separately-labeled "Player:" final line — I confirmed this directly by simulating the prompt construction with `node -e` (`recentMessages`'s last element is byte-for-byte identical to `playerMessage.text`). Practical impact: (a) `HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE=6` effectively only carries 5 PAST messages (the 6th is already a copy of the current message) — the context window is shorter than assumed; (b) the same information is given to the AI twice, wasting tokens unnecessarily (a paid API call) and potentially confusing the prompt structure. **Not a definite "wrong answer" bug** (the AI probably ignores the repetition) but the unnecessary cost/context waste is real. **Proposal:** fix `chat.js` so that when computing `recentMessages: history.slice(-HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE)`, the LAST element (the current `playerMessage`) is excluded — either `history.slice(-HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE, -1)` (since it's called after the push, the last element is playerMessage itself) or compute `recentMessages` BEFORE the push and push `playerMessage` separately. Either one fixes it without touching `buildPrompt` at all. A small/clear task (one line + one backend test) — assigned to the coder. **Fix:** `chat.js:200`'s `recentMessages: history.slice(-HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE)` was changed to `history.slice(-HISTORY_CONTEXT_SIZE, -1)` (the first of the two proposed options, `buildPrompt` untouched). **Test:** a new test was added to `aiGmFallback.test.js` — sending two consecutive messages and confirming that the second call's `recentMessages` passed to `generateAiNarration` does NOT contain the current message ("second message") but DOES contain the previous one ("first message") (using the mock's `require.cache` injection technique for capturing call arguments, the same pattern already in the file). Backend tests **189/189** (from 188 to 189, no regression). **PM note:** I re-simulated the fixed logic with `node -e` — the current message is no longer duplicated inside `recentMessages`. I also independently confirmed `npx vitest run` (189/189) myself. Push approved, pushed. Full independent tester QA is still pending.

98. **[2026-08-30] (Balance/design note, not auto-assigned — Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round) An upper limit of 1-22 is enforced on attributes at character creation (idea #13), but this limit is NEVER checked on level-up (`awardXp`) — a primary attribute can theoretically grow without bound.** `backend/services/leveling.js:35-37` — `character.attributes[classPrimaryAttribute] += 1` runs on every level-up with no upper-bound check at all (confirmed via grep/code reading, `MAX_ATTRIBUTE_VALUE=22` is used only in `character.js`'s `/create` route). There is a `MAX_LEVEL=20` (idea #31), meaning a character can level up at most 19 times — a primary attribute starting at 22 can theoretically reach 22+19=41. I tested the `abilityModifier` formula with `node -e`: modifier is +6 at a score of 22, +15 at a score of 41 — unusually high for a D20 system, and since `DIFFICULTY_CLASS=12` stays constant, this means nearly every attack becomes an automatic success/critical at high levels. **Practical risk is low:** with the formula `xpToNextLevel(level)=level*50`, reaching level 20 requires a total of ~9500 XP (each enemy kill gives +20 XP) — meaning it requires an extremely long session, roughly ~475 enemy kills, theoretically reachable with the current 4-encounter cyclical pool but nobody is expected to play a single session that long in practice. **Not a definite bug** — in D&D, characters growing stronger as they level up is already expected behavior, it's just an UPPER LIMIT that was never considered/documented, a design gap. **Proposal:** either (a) leave as-is (low practical risk, "feeling stronger" may already be a desired feeling), or (b) for consistency, add the `MAX_ATTRIBUTE_VALUE=22` upper bound to `awardXp` too (skip the +1 if the attribute is already at 22, XP/HP/mana gains unaffected). PM/user should prioritize — not auto-assigning.

97. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder) — (Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round) `nameMatchesText` (the shared name-matching function for both equip AND the new drop mechanic) only looks for the item's FULL name — when a player uses a natural/shortened reference ("I'm dropping my Sword"), the match silently fails.** I stood up a live server and confirmed directly with `curl`: with "Short Sword" in the inventory, sending "I'm dropping my Sword" correctly returns `true` for `isDropIntent`, but `resolveDrop`'s use of `nameMatchesText("Short Sword", "dropping my sword")` returns `false` (isolated test with `node -e`) — because the function looks for the FULL name ("short sword") or its consonant-softened form to appear verbatim in the text, and since the word "short" never appears, no match is established. Result: `resolveDrop` returns `null`, `resolveFreeformAction` drops nothing, and the system silently falls back to `resolveAction()`'s irrelevant flavor roll — the player thinks they "dropped my item" but it's still in their inventory (I directly saw in a live test that "Short Sword" stayed unchanged in `character.inventory`). **The same bug also affects `resolveEquip`** ("I'm equipping my Sword" also returns `false` the same way, separately confirmed with `node -e`) — since `nameMatchesText` is a shared function, both #93 (equip) and #96 (drop) inherit this weakness, it just hadn't been noticed until now (existing tests always used sentences with the FULL name, e.g. "I equip the Short Sword"). The item-name equivalent of the "write naturally" problem idea #90 solved for spell names. **Fix:** the literal+consonant-softening check inside `nameMatchesText` was extracted into a separate helper function named `variantMatchesText(lowerVariant, lowerText)`; `nameMatchesText` now first tries the FULL name, and if it doesn't match (when the name has more than one word), also tries the LAST word with the SAME check — "Short Sword" → "sword" → matches "my sword" (as a substring). Behavior for single-word names is unchanged. I confirmed with `node -e` both the original repro ("I'm dropping my Sword"/"I'm equipping my Sword" now `true`) and other multi-word items in the scene ("Gold Pouch"→"my Pouch", "Spider Silk"→"my Silk", "Old Shield"→"my Shield", "Dragon Scale"→"my Scale"), as well as the negative check (an unowned "my Helmet" still `false`). A new regression test covering both equip and drop in one test was added to `freeformCombat.test.js`. Backend tests showed no regression (188/188, including the new test). **PM note:** I did independent verification — I also confirmed 188/188 with `npx vitest run` myself, and additionally stood up a live server and re-ran the original repro ("I'm dropping my Sword"): the narration now returns "You dropped the Short Sword on the ground.", `roll:null` (correct, doesn't involve a D20), and "Short Sword" genuinely disappeared from `character.inventory`. Full independent tester QA is still pending (tester offline), this is only a pre-check.

96. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder), quick verification done by PM (tester offline, independent QA still pending) — (Creative cron, found by PM — PM role taken on this round) The freeform system has no item-drop mechanic at all — once `MAX_INVENTORY=30` is reached, the inventory stays permanently full.** `grep -rn "resolveDrop|isDropIntent|DROP_PATTERN|kind: \"drop\""` returned zero results in backend/services + backend/routes — the grid-era `/item/drop` route (`routes/scene.js`) was fully removed in Phase 12-C, and no freeform equivalent was ever added. `resolvePickup` (`freeformCombat.js:278-283`) correctly returns `{inventoryFull:true}` and refuses to add new items once `character.inventory.length >= MAX_INVENTORY` — this is correct/expected behavior, but there's no way (via chat) to empty the inventory. Practical risk is low (reaching 30 items requires a long session, the current loot pool is small/repetitive) but theoretically it's a permanent "stuck" state — in a long session a player could become unable to collect new loot, and the system itself never announces this or offers a solution (only an "your inventory is full" message). **Fix:** `DROP_PATTERN`/`isDropIntent` were added to `actionResolver.js` following idea #88's discipline (no bare short roots, conjugated forms + word-end-anchored imperative) — ("atıyorum"/"bırakıyorum" i.e. "throwing away"/"dropping" etc. — confirmed with `node -e` not to conflict with unrelated words like "atlıyorum"(jumping)/"satıyorum"(selling)/"yatıyorum"(lying down)/"anlatıyorum"(explaining), the WORD_START anchor already prevents the "atıyorum" substring from matching INSIDE words like "satıyorum"). `resolveDrop(character, sessionId, text)` was added to `freeformCombat.js` — using the SAME `nameMatchesText` pattern as `resolveEquip` (also tries Turkish consonant softening), it removes the name-matched item from the inventory and adds it to the scene's loot pool (as `{id,name}`, same as `resolvePickup`); if the name doesn't match (idea #93's discipline), it does NOT silently fall back to some other item. `kind:"drop"` narration text was added to `chat.js`. It's checked in `resolveFreeformAction` after equip, before pickup. 3 new tests were added to `actionResolver.test.js`, and 5 to `freeformCombat.test.js` (positive/negative coverage, an unowned item, an empty inventory, an end-to-end scenario of filling MAX_INVENTORY then dropping and picking up again). Backend tests showed no regression (187/187, including the new tests). **PM note:** I did an independent quick verification with `node -e` — "I'm dropping the Sword"/"I'm throwing away the Potion"/"Drop the Shield" correctly trigger, "I'm jumping onto the horse"/"I'm selling the item"/"I'm throwing the stone" [different verb "fırlat"] return false (no false positives). `npx vitest run` 187/187 confirmed. Full independent tester QA is still pending (tester offline), this is only a pre-check.

95. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder, took on the creative cron role and found this myself via code scanning — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The "-ip/-ıp/-up/-üp" conjunctive suffix used for verb chaining in Turkish, extremely common in natural speech ("I drink the potion and heal up", "I grab the gold and run", "I equip the sword and fight"), was covered by NONE of the four intent patterns in `actionResolver.js` (PICKUP/CONSUME/EQUIP, and partially STR) — I confirmed this directly with `node -e`: `isConsumeIntent("İksiri içip iyileşiyorum")` → `false`, `isPickupIntent("Altını alıp kaçıyorum")` → `false`, `isEquipIntent("Kılıcı kuşanıp savaşıyorum")` → `false` (STR_PATTERN's "vur"/"sald[ıi]r" were already bare roots so they already caught it, only "kır" was missing). This directly contradicted the project's "write naturally" promise (the same rationale idea #90 used to expand spell aliases) — when a player constructed a very natural sentence (chaining one action into the next), the system produced no mechanical result at all. **Fix:** `"alıp"`/`"kaldırıp"` were added to PICKUP_PATTERN, `"içip"` to CONSUME_PATTERN, `"kuşanıp"`/`"giyip"`/`"takıp"` to EQUIP_PATTERN, and `"kırıp"` to STR_PATTERN — all safe since they're roots of ≥3 letters (idea #88's discipline: a short bare root is never added). I additionally tested false-positive risk with `node -e`: "takip ediyorum" (takip≠takıp, "following"≠"and grab") and "alıcı bekliyorum" (alıcı≠alıp, "waiting for a buyer"≠"and grab") still return `false`, and "kaldırımda yürüyorum" ("walking on the sidewalk", idea #88) showed no regression. 2 new tests were added to `actionResolver.test.js` (positive coverage + negative/false-positive check). Backend tests showed no regression (179/179, including the new tests).

**Additional note (found during the same scan):** items #18, #26, #71, #81 have become inapplicable now that the tactical grid (`TacticalGrid.tsx`, `backend/routes/scene.js`) was fully removed in Phase 12-C — confirmed via `grep`/`ls` and marked "[x] NO LONGER APPLICABLE" in TASKS.md (below, original text preserved).

The new test (an "no enemy took damage" check via HP snapshot comparison) is well designed, covers the real regression. **#86:** `aria-live="polite"` was correctly added to the `restartError` `<p>` in `GameOverScreen.tsx`, the new test correctly checks the attribute. **Result: both approved, no additional issues found.**

94. **[2026-08-30] [x] FIXED (coder, my own finding + my own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The SAME bug class idea #93 fixed in `resolveEquip` was also present in `findTargetEnemy`, used by `resolveAttack`/`resolveCast` — I noticed this myself (not a tester finding). `backend/services/freeformCombat.js` — `const named = state.enemies.find(...); return named ?? state.enemies[0] ?? null;` — even if the name in the text matched NONE of the enemies in the current encounter, it silently fell back to `state.enemies[0]` (the first enemy). In single-enemy encounters (3/4 of the pool) there's no harm/ambiguity anyway, but in a multi-enemy encounter like "Skeleton Graveyard" (Skeleton Warrior + Skeleton Archer), when the player typed a nonexistent/already-dead name ("I attack the Dragon" or the name of an already-killed enemy), the system silently attacked an unrelated enemy. **Fix:** `findTargetEnemy` now only falls back when there's a name mismatch AND only a single enemy (no ambiguity); with 2+ enemies, a name mismatch returns `null` (no mechanical result is produced, falls back to plain narration) — consistent with the same fix `resolveEquip` got in #93. A new test was added to `backend/tests/freeformCombat.test.js` confirming that progressing to the Skeleton Graveyard and attacking with a nonexistent enemy name deals no damage to any enemy. Backend tests showed no regression (178/178, including the new test). Also verified live with curl: in a single-enemy (Goblin) encounter, attacking with a wrong name still (as expected) falls back to the single enemy — the behavior change only applies to the multi-enemy case.

93. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** Formally addressed now that Phase 12 is complete — the equip fallback bug the tester found during Phase 12-C-prep QA: when an unowned item was requested, `resolveEquip` silently swapped in a DIFFERENT item (the first equippable one) instead. `backend/services/freeformCombat.js:229` — `const item = equippable.find(...) ?? equippable[0];` — if the name in the text matched NONE of the inventory's items, it silently fell back to `equippable[0]` (the first equippable item). The tester had confirmed this live: while the sword was equipped, they said "I put on my Helmet" (not owned at all) — the system silently UNEQUIPPED the sword. The text at least says which item was affected so it's not entirely silent, but it can lead to unwanted disarmament mid-combat. **Proposal:** stay consistent with the pattern `resolveAttack`/`resolvePickup` already use — return `null` instead of falling back to `equippable[0]` when there's no match (produce no mechanical result, let `resolveFreeformAction` fall through to other intents/pure narration). A small/clear task, both coder and tester are free since Phase 12 is complete.

**Fix:** the `?? equippable[0]` fallback in `resolveEquip()` was removed entirely — it now returns `null` if the name doesn't match, producing no mechanical result. While doing this, it turned out that an existing test ("I equip the Short Sword") was actually passing WITHOUT any REAL name match, purely THANKS TO this fallback bug, due to Turkish consonant softening ("Kılıç"+"-ı"→"Kılıcı") — the test broke once the fallback was removed. This showed the name-matching itself needed fixing too: a small helper called `nameMatchesText()` was added — if the item name's last letter is a hard consonant (p/ç/t/k), it's also tried once more with the corresponding soft form (b/c/d/ğ) — both a genuine name match and common conjugated usage (like `Kılıcı`, and in the future `Topuzu`) now work correctly.

**Test:** 2 new tests — confirming that when an unowned item is requested (the tester's exact repro: while the sword is equipped, "I put on my Helmet"), the equipped sword no longer CHANGES, AND that a genuine consonant-softened name match ("I equip the Short Sword") still works correctly. Backend tests **176/176** (174 previous + 2 new), no regression.

**Live verification (real running `server.js`, repeating the tester's EXACT repro):** I created a real character and sent "I equip the Short Sword" — it genuinely equipped (`equipped:true`, consonant-softened matching works). Then I repeated the tester's original repro: "I put on my Helmet" (an unowned item) — real output: NO "you equipped/unequipped" statement at all, the sword is still `equipped:true` (not mistakenly removed). Temporary server/files cleaned up.

**Tester verification (independent, 2026-08-26):** Backend **176/176**. I repeated my own original repro exactly (while the sword is equipped, unowned "I put on my Helmet") — the sword now genuinely STAYS equipped, no silent disarmament. I also confirmed the consonant-softened matching ("I equip my Short Sword") matches correctly. **A small additional observation (not a bug, a pre-existing limitation, outside this fix's scope):** the item name matches ONLY in its full ("Short Sword") or full+consonant-softened ("Short Sword"+suffix) form — if the player drops the adjective and just says "I equip my Sword" (a very common shortening in real speech), there's no match, and silently nothing happens (at least now nothing WRONG happens either — previously this case also fell back to `equippable[0]`). This behavior was the same BEFORE the fix too (name matching was always a full-text substring), outside this fix's scope, low priority, can be noted as a separate idea.

**Idea #93 fully verified — all known findings from Phase 12 are now closed.**

92. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session) — ⚠️ IMPORTANT — Ever since the grid was removed in Phase 12-C, `/chat` had NO dead-character (hp≤0) check at all, relying entirely on the frontend to show GameOverScreen.** The grid's (now-deleted) `requireOwnedCharacter()` function in `routes/scene.js` checked `character.hp.current<=0` on every action and returned 400 — this check disappeared TOGETHER WITH the grid, and freeform has no equivalent at all. NONE of `resolveAttack`/`resolveCast`/`resolveConsume`/`resolvePickup`/`resolveEquip` in `backend/services/freeformCombat.js` check `character.hp.current>0` (confirmed via grep — the only `hp.current<=0` reference in the file is in `resolveEnemyRetaliation`, and that's only to prevent a second retaliation within the SAME message, it doesn't prevent the character from STARTING a new action). Now that `/chat` is the game's ONLY interface (the grid's old protective role is entirely gone), this observation, previously noted as "low priority, low practical risk" (during the tester's Phase 12-C-prep QA), is now a real/significant gap: a dead character (hp=0) could attack, gain XP, or even "revive" silently via a Heal cast with the formula `Math.min(max, 0+8)` through a direct API call or a race condition — there's no server-side barrier at all, relying entirely on client-side (GameOverScreen) protection (contrary to the "never trust client-side alone" principle this project has followed throughout). **Proposal:** add a check to `/chat`'s `POST /`, BEFORE `resolveFreeformAction` is called — if `character && character.hp.current<=0`, skip the mechanical action entirely (instead of returning 400, given chat's freeform/roleplay nature, it may be more fitting to just return a narration message like "Your character is dead, you must start a new adventure." — without breaking the API). A small/clear task, can be picked up once the coder is free.

**Fix:** an early-return check was added to `chat.js`'s `POST /`, BEFORE `resolveFreeformAction` is called: if `character && character.hp.current<=0`, it returns a fixed narration message ("Your character is dying and can no longer act... You must start a new adventure.") with no AI call/mechanical resolution at all, `roll:null`. This both closes the security gap and prevents a dead character from consuming the shared hourly budget with unnecessary AI calls. As a defensive layer, the `if (!character) return null;` check at the very start of `resolveFreeformAction` in `freeformCombat.js` was also extended to include `character.hp.current<=0` (chat.js's early return is the main barrier, this is a second layer of protection).

**Test:** in three existing tests (idea #87's full-turn test, the XP/encounter-transition test, and the "no retaliation once the last enemy dies" test), it was noticed that enemy retaliation now deals REAL damage — with the fixed maximum-damage mock (0.99), every retaliation also lands a critical hit, so the player could die before the test's intended scenario completed; a fix was added to these three tests deliberately bringing the character's HP up high (not a bug, proof that retaliation genuinely works). 2 new tests were added: confirming a dead character can produce no mechanical result with attack/spell/item/equip, gains no XP, their inventory doesn't change, and they can't even "revive" via Heal (mana also isn't spent). Backend tests **174/174** (172 previous + 2 new), no regression.

**Live verification (real running `server.js`, genuinely killing a REAL character as the PM requested):** I created a real character and sent repeated attacks — **on the 3rd attack, the character genuinely DIED** (from enemy retaliation, `hp:0`). I then sent another attack with the SAME (now dead) character — real output: `"Your character is dying and can no longer act... You must start a new adventure."`, `roll:null`, `character.hp.current:0` (unchanged), `character.xp:0` (no gain) — the mechanical action was genuinely fully blocked. Temporary server/files cleaned up.

90. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA)** `detectSpellId()` only looked for the spell's FULL Turkish name ("Fireball"/"Heal") to appear verbatim in the text — natural but name-free phrases like "I'm healing myself" or "I'm setting it on fire" never triggered the spell at all. `actionResolver.js:90-101` — a deliberate choice the coder explicitly noted in their own comment (matching on distinctive/long names to avoid the short-root false-positive family) — but the cost of this is that it partially contradicts the project's Questsmith/AI Dungeon-inspired "write naturally, no menus/buttons" promise: a player has to know their spell's FULL NAME ("Heal") and type it verbatim, and equivalent but name-free natural phrases like "I'm bandaging my wounds"/"I'm treating myself" silently do nothing (neither spell nor attack — returns null and falls into a generic mock narration, the player may not even notice their spell didn't work). **Caution (to avoid repeating the short-root risk):** when expanding this, specific/multi-word phrases should be used again instead of single-letter/very-short roots (like idea #88's "kaldır" bug) (e.g. for Fireball, "flames"/"throw fire"/"I'm burning", for Heal, "I'm healing"/"cure"/"bandage my wounds" — roots of 4+ letters/uncommon words) — it's essential not to reverse the coder's recent choice and go back to bare short roots. A small-medium task, leaving it to the coder (may skip it this round if they wish, can also be picked up in a later cron round).

**Fix:** a `SPELL_ALIASES` map was added to `actionResolver.js` — for each spell, in addition to its full name, at least TWO-WORD/specific synonymous phrases: for Fireball, `"fire orb"`, `"flame ball"`, `"fire spell"`; for Heal, `"treat myself"` (Heal itself already matched as its full name in conjugated forms like "I'm healing", so it needed fewer additional aliases). `detectSpellId()` now checks both the full name and the alias list. Deliberately, NO single-word/short root was ADDED (to avoid repeating idea #88's "kaldır" bug) — each alias is at least two words, low risk of an accidental standalone collision.

**Test:** 2 new tests were added to `actionResolver.test.js` — confirming the expanded synonymous phrases return the correct spellId AND that unrelated sentences ("I see a red flame", "I'm sitting next to the fire") still return `null` (proof the short-root error wasn't repeated). Backend tests **291/291** (289 previous + 2 new), no regression.

**Verification:** 10 scenarios were tested in isolation with Node (5 positive synonym phrases + 3 previously-working full-name examples + 2 negative/unrelated sentences) — all gave the expected result. Since this is a pure text-matching function (no external state/DB/AI call), a separate live-server verification was not deemed necessary, node-level verification + the backend test suite are sufficient evidence.

89. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** Phase 12-B's new `HeaderHud` had no `aria-live` — now that the grid is hidden by default, this bar is the PRIMARY way to see HP/Mana, but a screen reader never heard the change on every attack/heal. `HeaderHud.tsx:13-16` — the root `<div>` has a static `aria-label` (name/HP/level, mana not included) but no `aria-live` (confirmed via grep).
