84. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The `pgSmokeTest.js` added in idea #72 had no safety check preventing it from being accidentally run against the PROD `DATABASE_URL`. `backend/scripts/pgSmokeTest.js` — it ran directly whenever `DATABASE_URL` was set AND `VITEST` was not (confirmed, lines 15-22); in CI it safely runs against GitHub Actions' own temporary `postgres:16` container (this was idea #72's purpose) — but the script itself was completely unaware of this context, and would run the same way against ANY `DATABASE_URL`. If a developer (coder/tester/user) temporarily put a real Render Postgres connection string in their `.env` to investigate a prod issue locally and absent-mindedly ran `node scripts/pgSmokeTest.js` — the script pulls ALL prod rows via `getAllCharacters()`/`getAllScenes()`/`getAllChatHistories()`/`getAllSessions()`, filters them in memory, creates its own test row (with id `pg-smoke-<timestamp>`), and DELETES it. The risk of the test ID colliding with real nanoid character IDs is low, but the script performed no "am I connecting to prod?" check at all — its runnability outside CI was an entirely silent assumption. **Proposal:** add a safety belt to the script — either require the `GITHUB_ACTIONS` environment variable (automatically set by GH Actions itself) ("only run in CI"), or reject a connection string matching a known prod host (a Render-specific pattern like `dpg-...`, or require an explicit approval flag `ALLOW_PG_SMOKE_TEST=true`). A small/clear task.

**Fix:** a new early-exit check was added to `pgSmokeTest.js` right after the `DATABASE_URL`/`VITEST` checks: if `GITHUB_ACTIONS` (a variable GitHub Actions itself automatically sets on every workflow run) is not set AND `ALLOW_PG_SMOKE_TEST !== "true"`, the script exits with `process.exit(1)` and a clear explanation, without attempting any DB connection. This implements BOTH of the PM's two proposed options (requiring GITHUB_ACTIONS or an approval flag) — it runs automatically without issue in CI, and a developer who deliberately wants to run it locally can bypass with `ALLOW_PG_SMOKE_TEST=true`. The code change is only in this file, the CI workflow (`test.yml`) was not touched — `GITHUB_ACTIONS` being automatically set on every step is a behavior confirmed in GitHub's official documentation. Backend tests **266/266** (this script is outside vitest, not included in `npm test`, no regression risk).

**Live verification:** locally, I ran the script with a fake `DATABASE_URL` (`postgres://fake:fake@localhost:5432/fake`) but WITHOUT setting `GITHUB_ACTIONS` — it stopped with `exit code: 1` and the new safety message, without attempting any DB connection (proof: the scenario of accidentally connecting to a real prod is now blocked from the start). I then re-ran it with the same fake `DATABASE_URL` plus `ALLOW_PG_SMOKE_TEST=true` — this time it PASSED the new check and genuinely reached `dbPostgres.js`'s `init()`, failing with `ECONNREFUSED` (no real Postgres on localhost:5432) — meaning the bypass flag genuinely works, it skips the check but attempts to connect to the fake host (no false positive). Finally I watched it on real GitHub Actions (CI, where `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set automatically) with `gh run watch` — the workflow is **GREEN**, the line `pgSmokeTest: all CRUD checks passed against real Postgres (engine=postgres).` still appears, meaning the new safety check did NOT BREAK normal operation in CI.

I ran a separate Node script implementing the EXACT SAME logic as `handleEndTurn()`'s outer `try/catch/finally` against that now-down server — real output: `pending: false` (button doesn't stay disabled, no softlock), `error: "fetch failed"` (a real error message now reaches the UI, not silently swallowed). 82. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** `handleRestart()` had no `try/catch` at all — if "Restart" failed (a network error, Render cold start), the player was silently stuck on the Game Over screen or idea #33's confirmation box, seeing no feedback at all. `App.tsx:191-203` — `async function handleRestart() { await resetSession(); setCharacter(null); ... }` (confirmed via grep, there was no `try`/`catch` at all). If `resetSession()` (the backend's `POST /character/reset`) is rejected, `await` throws and the function exits there — NO subsequent `setState` is called, and the player would see no change on screen at all. This is exactly the kind of scenario that can happen with Render's cold-start/network-fluctuation scenario (which idea #4 handled but ONLY for the initial load, not this button) — and moreover, the `ErrorBoundary` added by idea #55 does NOT CATCH this (React error boundaries don't cover async errors in event handlers, only render-time synchronous errors). **Proposal:** add `try{...}catch(e){ /* set an error state, show the user "Connection error, try again" */ }` to `handleRestart` — consistent with one of the existing `NetworkError`/error-display patterns (idea #37, #4). A small/clear task.

**Fix:** `restarting`/`restartError` state was added to `App.tsx`, and `handleRestart()` was wrapped with `try/catch/finally` — on error, `restartError` is set, and in `finally`, `restarting` always returns to `false` regardless of outcome (the same pattern as idea #45's `continuing`/`continueError`). Both call sites of `handleRestart` were updated: (1) `restarting`/`restartError` props were added to `GameOverScreen.tsx` — the button is disabled while `restarting` and shows "Restarting..." text, and if there's an error, it's shown below via `<p className="error">`; (2) the "Yes, Delete"/"Cancel" buttons in the character panel's `restart-confirm` block (idea #33) are also disabled while `restarting`, with the error message shown below the same confirmation box — the confirmation box does NOT CLOSE on error (only inside the `try` block does `setConfirmingRestart(false)` get called on success), so the user can try again. A permanent regression test was added to `App.test.tsx`: confirming the Game Over screen doesn't disappear when `resetSession()` is rejected, the error message appears, the "Restart" button does NOT STAY disabled (proof it doesn't silently get stuck), and that on a second attempt (mock succeeds) the flow completes normally. Frontend tests **85/85** (84 previous + 1 new), `oxlint` clean, `tsc -b && vite build` clean.

**Live verification (a real network error, not simulated):** I started a temporary backend server and created a character, then genuinely TERMINATED the server process (`taskkill` with the Windows PID, the freed port confirmed via `netstat`). I ran a separate Node script implementing the EXACT SAME logic as `api.ts`'s `resetSession()`→`request()` (fetch + try/catch/finally, an exact copy of `handleRestart`'s code path) against that now-DOWN server — real output: `restarting: false`, `restartError: "fetch failed"`, the text to be shown in the UI: `"fetch failed Try again."` — meaning that when the server is genuinely down, the `restarting` state doesn't silently get stuck at `true` (it always returns to `false`, thanks to `finally`) and a real error text reaches the user. Temporary server/files cleaned up.

79. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** On a rate-limit (429) error, the user didn't know how long to wait — the `Retry-After`/`RateLimit-*` headers sent via `standardHeaders: true` were never read anywhere. `backend/services/publicRateLimit.js:15` has `standardHeaders: true` (making express-rate-limit add `RateLimit-*` and usually `Retry-After` seconds info to its 429 response) but the `request()` function in `frontend/src/api.ts` (including the error display idea #37 added to `ChatPanel`) only read the `data.error` text, never looking at the HTTP response headers at all (confirmed via grep). Thanks to idea #37/#36 the user now sees the message "Too many requests sent, please wait a bit and try again." but didn't know EXACTLY how many seconds to wait — how much is "a bit"? **Proposal:** in `api.ts`'s `request()`, on a 429, read `res.headers.get('Retry-After')` and append it to the error message (e.g. "Too many requests sent, try again in 23 seconds." — no need to change the backend's own message, it can be enriched on the frontend side, or dynamically added to the backend's `message` function too). A small/clear task.

**Fix:** a block was added to the `!res.ok` branch of `request()` in `frontend/src/api.ts` that, when `res.status===429`, reads `res.headers.get('Retry-After')`, converts it to a number, and if valid (`Number.isFinite`, `>0`), appends `" (Try again in N seconds.)"` to the existing error message. The backend's own message (`message` in `publicRateLimit.js`) was not changed — only enriched on the frontend side; I confirmed in `express-rate-limit@8.6.2`'s source (`setRetryAfterHeader`) that the `Retry-After` header is always set on a 429 in SECONDS (`resetSeconds.toString()`), independent of the `standardHeaders` setting. Frontend tests **83/83** passed, `oxlint` clean.

**Live verification (real running `server.js`, with `PUBLIC_RATE_LIMIT_MAX=2`):** I started a temporary server and sent 3 requests in a row to `POST /character/roll-stats` (via curl) — the 3rd request genuinely returned **429** and the response headers showed **`Retry-After: 60`** (the full window duration, since the first attempt was right at the start of the window). I then ran a separate Node script implementing the EXACT SAME logic as `request()` in `api.ts` (fetch + header reading + message concatenation) against the same live server — real output: `"Too many requests sent, please wait a bit and try again. (Try again in 49 seconds.)"` (49 this time since the request landed a bit further into the window — proof `Retry-After` is genuinely read dynamically/correctly, not a fixed number). Temporary server and QA script cleaned up.

74. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** `callGemini()` has no timeout at all — if the Gemini API hangs/responds extremely slowly, the request could hang indefinitely, meaning the project's most fundamental design principle, "silently fall back to mock" (since Phase 2), doesn't work in this scenario. `callGemini()` at `backend/services/aiGm.js:20-34` — the `await model.generateContent(prompt)` call has no `AbortController`/timeout mechanism at all (confirmed via grep). The existing error handling (`try{...}catch{fall back to mock}` in `narrationService.js`) only catches a REJECTED promise — if Google's API delays responding/never responds (a network issue, a temporary Google-side slowdown, etc.), the promise neither resolves nor rejects, and `await` waits indefinitely. In this case that player's request (chat/attack/cast/move/intro) hangs — the loading indicator idea #53 added spins forever, never falls back to mock, the player gets stuck (Render's own request timeout might eventually kick in, but that could take not seconds but possibly minutes, unpredictable). **Proposal:** add a reasonable timeout to `callGemini()` (e.g. 15-20 seconds) — check whether the Gemini SDK has its own `requestOptions.timeout` support (use it if present, check the type definitions in node_modules), otherwise add a manual timeout with the `Promise.race([model.generateContent(prompt), timeoutPromise])` pattern, throwing an error once the time is up so the existing mock-fallback mechanism kicks in. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** first I checked the SDK's (`@google/generative-ai` v0.24.1) type definitions — `getGenerativeModel(modelParams, requestOptions?: RequestOptions)`'s second parameter already supported a `RequestOptions.timeout?: number` field, so a manual `Promise.race` wasn't needed. An `AI_TIMEOUT_MS=15000` constant was added to `aiGm.js`, and `{ timeout: AI_TIMEOUT_MS }` was added as the second argument to the `getGenerativeModel()` call in `callGemini()`. When the timeout occurs, the SDK throws `GoogleGenerativeAIAbortError` (a regular `Error` subclass) — the existing `try{...}catch{fall back to mock}` structure in `narrationService.js`/`character.js` catches it the same way without needing any changes. Backend tests showed no regression (266/266).

**Live verification (with a real `GEMINI_API_KEY`, using the PM's suggested "add artificial delay → verify → revert" method):** I temporarily set `AI_TIMEOUT_MS` to `1` (1ms) and triggered a real `/character/intro` request — the server log genuinely showed the error **`Request aborted when fetching ... This operation was aborted`** (an error COMPLETELY DIFFERENT from the known quota/billing 429 — proof the SDK's timeout mechanism genuinely triggered), and the response continued smoothly with `source:"mock"` (no hanging). After verification, `AI_TIMEOUT_MS` was reverted to `15000` (confirmed in `git diff` that only the permanent 15s value remains). The test character was cleaned up with `/character/reset`.

73. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** There's no `maxOutputTokens` limit at all on Gemini calls — the prompt asks for "3-5 sentences" but this is only a SOFT instruction, with no hard limit if the model doesn't comply. `backend/services/aiGm.js:26-27` — the `client.getGenerativeModel({ model: MODEL_NAME })` and `model.generateContent(prompt)` calls never pass `generationConfig` at all (confirmed via grep). LLMs may not follow instructions 100% (especially given that the system prompt already says to treat "Player:" lines as untrusted, the model can sometimes produce a much longer response than expected) — without a hard limit, a single narration message could come back much longer than expected: (a) it breaks the game's terse/atmospheric narration style, (b) `chatHistoryLimit`'s (idea #6) 200-message limit caps the NUMBER of messages but not the SIZE of a single message, which could bloat the data written to the DB, (c) a longer response = a slower/more expensive API call, making the already-limited `AI_HOURLY_LIMIT=30` budget even more precious. **Proposal:** add a reasonable hard upper limit like `generationConfig: { maxOutputTokens: 300 }` to `getGenerativeModel()` (or `generateContent()`) (more than enough for a 3-5 sentence Turkish narration, but prevents a genuine "runaway" response). A small/clear task — since it needs to be tested with a real AI call (invisible in mock), the coder should verify it live or locally with a real `GEMINI_API_KEY`.

**Fix:** a `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS=300` constant was added to `aiGm.js`, and `generationConfig: { maxOutputTokens: 300 }` was added to `callGemini()`'s `getGenerativeModel()` call (a field defined exactly this way in the SDK's (`@google/generative-ai` v0.24.1) `GenerationConfig` type — checked in `node_modules`). Since this one call site is shared by both `generateAiNarration` and `generateOpeningStory`, it covers both. Backend tests showed no regression (266/266, `aiGm.js` was already never called directly in any test — the mock/fallback path is what's tested).

**Live verification (with a real `GEMINI_API_KEY`, local dev server) — in the interest of honesty:** I created a real character and triggered `/character/intro` — **it fell back to mock due to a quota/billing issue (429 "prepayment credits depleted")**, a known and recurring user-side quota restriction since previous phases (Phase 2/3/8), unrelated to this change. But I confirmed in the server log that the real request reached Google's API and got a **429 (billing) back** — if the `generationConfig` field had been INCORRECTLY formatted, the API would have instead returned a **400 (bad request)**, meaning the request body (including the new `generationConfig` field) was accepted as VALID by the API, just couldn't be processed due to quota. I also confirmed this by reading the SDK's TypeScript type definitions (`GenerationConfig.maxOutputTokens?: number`, exactly the shape I used). Measuring the real response length with a fully successful generation isn't currently possible without a key that has available quota — can be retried if a new/refilled-quota key becomes available (same note as previous phases). Test character cleaned up with `/character/reset`.

72. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The CI pipeline (idea #59) always falls back to SQLite — `dbPostgres.js`'s real Postgres behavior is never exercised in any test, even though prod uses EXACTLY this file. Even `backend/data/db.js`'s own comment states this explicitly: "Tests (VITEST=true) ALWAYS fall back to SQLite (:memory:) even if DATABASE_URL is set" — a grep for `dbPostgres` in `backend/tests/` returns zero results, no test file ever imports/exercises this module. So the "CI green/red on every push" safety net idea #59 brought never covers the code path (Postgres) that REALLY runs in prod — a SQL syntax error in `dbPostgres.js`, an incorrect parameter binding, or a behavioral difference from SQLite (e.g. type conversion, the `RETURNING` pattern) could pass CI green but break silently (or openly) in prod. **Proposal:** add a `postgres:` service container to the backend job in `.github/workflows/test.yml` (a very common/well-documented pattern in GitHub Actions), set `DATABASE_URL` to point to that container, and add a second job/step that re-runs the tests WITHOUT the `VITEST` flag (or in a separate mode that skips the SQLite forcing) — at minimum, a minimal smoke test verifying `dbPostgres.js`'s basic CRUD functions (upsert/get/delete) once against a real Postgres would be enough. A medium-sized task (CI + a test file + possibly requires revisiting `db.js`'s test-forcing logic) but a well-known/mechanical GitHub Actions pattern.

**Fix:** a `postgres:16` service container (with a health-check, `5432:5432`) was added to the `backend` job in `.github/workflows/test.yml`. The new `backend/scripts/pgSmokeTest.js` — deliberately run with plain `node`, NOT `vitest run` (since the VITEST env variable isn't set, `data/db.js` genuinely selects `dbPostgres.js`) — verifies a full CRUD cycle against a real Postgres: `init`/`ping`, upsert for all 4 tables (including confirming the row doesn't duplicate when the same id is written a second time + the data genuinely updates), a round-trip via `getAll*`, `getStaleSessions`, and finally that the `delete*` functions genuinely delete. A `db.engine==="postgres"` assertion prevents the script from accidentally falling back to SQLite and passing as a false positive. It's added as a separate step AFTER the `npm test` step, within the same job (no need for an extra job/checkout/npm ci repetition).

**Live verification (on REAL GitHub Actions, via `gh run watch` — this round involved a genuine red/green cycle too, not hypothetical):** on the first push, `pgSmokeTest.js` genuinely went **RED**: `Error: The server does not support SSL connections` — `dbPostgres.js` requests SSL by default (`ssl: {rejectUnauthorized:false}`), but GitHub Actions' `postgres:16` service container doesn't support SSL (unlike prod/Render's managed Postgres, where it's not an issue). A `DATABASE_SSL: "false"` env was added to the `pgSmokeTest.js` step (an already-existing variable designed exactly for this scenario). On the second push, the workflow went **GREEN**, and the log line `pgSmokeTest: all CRUD checks passed against real Postgres (engine=postgres).` was confirmed — `db.engine` genuinely returned "postgres" (didn't accidentally fall back to SQLite), all CRUD assertions passed. The backend's normal `npm test` is also still green in the same job (the SQLite path showed no regression).

67. **[2026-08-26] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The `prefers-reduced-motion` media query is never used anywhere — none of the animations added during this session are toned down for users with motion sensitivity.** Zero results for `prefers-reduced-motion` project-wide in `frontend/src` (confirmed via grep). Quite a lot of animation has accumulated since Phase 11 onward: chat message fade-in (idea #37/Phase11), attack/spell/enemy-hit shake+flash+damage-popup (#39/#46/#47), tokens sliding between grid cells (Phase 11 polish), the spinner during AI calls (#53), and the dice-roll animation in `CharacterCreation.tsx` (a random number flashes every 60ms, for `ROLL_ANIMATION_MS`). None of these are toned down/disabled for a user with a vestibular disorder or who has turned on "reduce motion" in their OS preferences — a standard web accessibility expectation (WCAG 2.3.3) isn't met. **Proposal:** add a general override to `App.css`, like `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; } }` (covers most CSS-based animations in one go) — since the JS `setInterval`-based dice animation in `CharacterCreation.tsx` can't be toned down with CSS, a `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches` check may be needed there to skip the interval and show the result directly. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** a general "kill switch" was added to `index.css`: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *,*::before,*::after { animation-duration/animation-iteration-count/transition-duration: ...!important; scroll-behavior: auto!important; } }` (covers ALL the CSS animations from this session — chat fade-in, damage shake/flash, token sliding, spinner — in one go, no need to hunt down individual selectors). A `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches` check was added to `handleRoll()` in `CharacterCreation.tsx` — if the preference is set, the `setInterval`-based fake-random animation is never started AND the `ROLL_ANIMATION_MS` (700ms) artificial wait is skipped, showing the real dice result directly as soon as it comes back from the API. Frontend tests showed no regression (83/83), `tsc -b`+`npm run lint` clean.

**Live verification (Playwright, emulating the real browser media preference with `page.emulateMedia({reducedMotion})`):** `.spinner`'s `animation-duration` measured at **`1e-05s`** (0.01ms, proof the `!important` override genuinely applies) under `reduce`, and the normal **`0.7s`** under `no-preference`. Dice animation: under `reduce`, the transition from click to the "Roll Again" text took **~116ms** (just the real network round-trip, no artificial delay at all), and under `no-preference` it took **~795ms** (consistent with the expected `ROLL_ANIMATION_MS=700` + network latency) — a clear and consistent difference, proving the JS-based animation is also correctly toned down. No console errors (a transient 502/network delay was seen on the first attempt, confirmed unrelated with a clean re-run).

62. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** A request with a missing/empty `X-Session-Id` header falls back to a shared/fixed `"default"` session ID — any client without the header (curl, a bot, a script) can access the same persistent "shared" character.** `backend/services/sessionId.js:5-9` — `return "default"` if there's no header (the comment in the code says this is deliberate, a convenience for `curl`/old tests). The real frontend (`session.ts`) ALWAYS generates and sends a `crypto.randomUUID()` (even if localStorage is blocked, it generates an in-memory UUID, never skips the header — confirmed via grep), meaning NORMAL browser users never fall into this shared bucket. But since this project is deployed with a "user-facing" goal, ANY outside client that doesn't send the header (a bot, a security scanner, a curious developer) can access the fixed/deterministic `"default"` session and create/manipulate a character there — a persistent, shared-by-everyone, never-reset "general" game state. This fixed string also carries a name-collision risk (if two different automated testing tools send a header-less request at the same time, they could corrupt each other's data). **Caution:** `backend/tests/scene.test.js` has ~23 direct references using exactly the `scenes.get("default")`/`scenes.get("default").x=...` pattern — the tests hardcode the literal `"default"` string as the key of the Map in `data/store.js` (setting up fixtures assuming header-less requests fall into this key). **Proposal:** instead of the fixed `"default"` in `sessionId.js`, use a `crypto.randomUUID()` constant (`FALLBACK_SESSION_ID`) generated ONCE when the module loads, AND export it from `module.exports`; replace all `"default"` references in `scene.test.js` with this exported constant (a mechanical but many-lines find-replace). Result: header-less requests still consistently fall into the same (single) session (tests don't break), but now it's an ID that can't be guessed without reading the code/changes from deploy to deploy — not a fixed/documentable "default" string. A small task but touches many test lines — should be done carefully, with all tests (`npm test`) confirmed passing.

**Fix:** a `DEFAULT_SESSION_ID` constant, generated once via `crypto.randomUUID()` when the module loads, was added to `services/sessionId.js` (and exported), used as the fallback instead of the fixed `"default"` string. The 23 `scenes.get("default")` references in `tests/scene.test.js` and the 1 `chatHistories.get("default")` reference in `tests/characterIntro.test.js` were updated to import and use `DEFAULT_SESSION_ID` (a bulk find-replace via node + individual verification). The relevant test name in `tests/sessionIsolation.test.js` also no longer claims a literal "default" (updated to correctly describe the behavior, no code change). Backend tests **267/267** (no regression).

**Live verification:** confirmed via `node -e` that `DEFAULT_SESSION_ID` is in a real UUID format (like `8d88b483-a3cc-4b9e-83ce-ab3950b5b7da`). Against the local dev server with curl: a header-less `POST /character/create` + a header-less `GET /character` returned the **SAME** character (the fallback session is still consistent/persistent — behavior unchanged), but now this session's ID can't be guessed without reading the code. Test character cleaned up with `/character/reset`.

**Fix:** before adding it to CI, running `npm run lint` locally produced a real ERROR (not just a warning): in `CharacterCard.tsx`, the `useItem()` function from `api.ts` was called inside `handleUse`, but since its name started with "use...", oxlint's `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` rule mistook it for a React Hook and incorrectly errored that it was "called somewhere that isn't a hook" (not a real hook, a plain API call — a naming collision). `useItem` was renamed to `consumeItem` in `api.ts` (its single caller, `CharacterCard.tsx`, was also updated) — `npm run lint` is now clean with **exit 0** (4 pre-existing warnings remain, no errors, they don't break CI). An `npm run lint` step was added to the `frontend` job in `test.yml`, before `npm run build`. Frontend tests showed no regression (83/83), `tsc -b` clean. Live Playwright verification: the renamed `consumeItem()` call returned 200 on a real potion use, the item genuinely dropped from the inventory, no console errors.

**Live verification (on REAL GitHub Actions, via `gh run watch`):** I watched the workflow after the push — the `Run npm run lint` step in the `frontend` job genuinely ran and completed **green** (`backend` also stayed green, unchanged). 4 pre-existing warnings (2× `no-unsafe-optional-chaining` in a test file, 2× `set-state-in-effect`) appeared in the Actions' "ANNOTATIONS" section but didn't break the job — these are out of scope for this idea (not a real error, may need a behavioral review), left untouched.

58. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** `trust proxy` is never set in Express, so real-IP-based rate-limiting behind Render's proxy (Cloudflare) is likely behaving incorrectly. There are zero results for `app.set("trust proxy", ...)` in `backend/server.js` (confirmed via grep). I checked the prod backend with curl: the response headers show `Server: cloudflare` and `rndr-id` — meaning requests pass through Cloudflare + Render's own proxy layer, and the `req.ip` Express sees is likely not the real client IP but the proxy's internal IP. `express-rate-limit` (v8), used by `services/publicRateLimit.js`, uses `req.ip` as the key by default — without `trust proxy` correctly set, this either (a) sees ALL players as the same IP/bucket, dropping them into one shared 20/min limit (one player's spam affects EVERYONE), or (b) the library's validation layer could behave incorrectly/inconsistently since the `X-Forwarded-For` header comes from an untrusted proxy chain. The tests so far (idea #2/#36/#40) were always done from a SINGLE test machine, so this issue may not have surfaced (from a single IP, correct keying and wrong/shared keying look the SAME). **Proposal:** add `app.set("trust proxy", 1)` (or whatever value Render's documentation recommends) to `server.js`, matching Render's proxy depth, and verify live from two DIFFERENT IPs (or by manually altering the `X-Forwarded-For` header) that rate-limiting is genuinely counted separately. A small/clear task, but with an important effect (rate-limiting genuinely working IP-based).

**Fix:** `app.set("trust proxy", 1)` was added to `server.js` (the recommended value for Render's standard single-hop web service setup). Backend tests showed no regression (267/267) — since route tests never import `server.js` via `buildApp()` (the same reason as idea #23/#34/#56), this was verified live with curl.

**Live verification (local dev server, with two separate `X-Forwarded-For` values, a BEFORE/AFTER comparison):** WITHOUT `trust proxy` (temporarily reverted via `git stash`): I exhausted the budget (limit 3, `PUBLIC_RATE_LIMIT_MAX=3`) with `X-Forwarded-For: 1.1.1.1`, then immediately sent a request with a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT `X-Forwarded-For: 9.9.9.9` — **still returned 429** (confirmed `RateLimit-Limit: 3` in the header), meaning the claimed-different IPs were sharing the SAME (proxy/socket) bucket — the bug the finding claimed was confirmed live exactly. Then I brought back the fix with `git stash pop` (with the default limit 20) and tested again: 20 requests with `X-Forwarded-For: 3.3.3.3` got 200, requests 21-22 got 429 — immediately afterward, a request sent with a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT `X-Forwarded-For: 4.4.4.4` returned **200** (got its own fresh budget, entirely unaffected by IP-A's exhaustion). This definitively proves the fix genuinely works. Note: in prod, real client IPs are correctly appended to `X-Forwarded-For` by Render's edge (what I emulated with curl) — real multi-user verification is only possible with traffic from genuinely different IPs, but the mechanism itself (trust proxy correctly processing `X-Forwarded-For`) was definitively verified.

57. **[2026-08-25] 🚨 CRITICAL — ⚠️ Not a code change, requires a user action — Render's free Postgres plan is AUTOMATICALLY DELETED after 90 days, this project has never addressed this so far.** Idea #1 (fixed in Phase 9) only addressed the risk of "the free Postgres slowly hitting the 1GB storage limit" — but Render has a separate and much more serious platform policy: free-tier Postgres databases are completely DELETED by Render 90 DAYS AFTER CREATION (unless upgraded/recreated) — independent of size/usage, based purely on age. The `dnd-game-db` (plan: free) in `render.yaml` is subject to this policy — the elapsed time since the project was first deployed (Phase 7-B) is unknown, but this fact has NEVER been documented in TASKS.md/AGENTS.md/render.yaml, never tracked. If the database gets deleted around/on day 90 (Render generally sends an advance email warning), ALL characters/scenes/chat histories would be PERMANENTLY lost (no backup/migration mechanism exists on the code side). **This is not in the coder/tester's scope** — it requires either (a) upgrading to a paid Postgres plan on the Render dashboard, or (b) creating a new free Postgres and manually migrating the data before 90 days elapse (or recreating it periodically), an account/billing decision. **Proposal:** the PM/user should check `dnd-game-db`'s creation date on the Render dashboard, note the 90-day window on a calendar, or consider moving to a paid plan now.

56. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The backend's only error middleware catches only the CORS error — a genuine unexpected server error (500) falls through to Express's default handler, breaking the consistent `{error:"..."}` format and potentially leaking a stack trace.** `backend/server.js:84-89` — `app.use((err, _req, res, next) => {...})` handles ONLY the `err.message === "CORS: origin not allowed."` case, calling `next(err)` for every other error, leaving it to Express's own default error handler (confirmed via grep — no other 4-argument middleware exists). `NODE_ENV` is never set anywhere (zero grep results in `render.yaml`/`package.json`/`server.js`) — Express's default behavior adds a stack trace (file paths, line numbers) to the error response when `NODE_ENV!=="production"`, and even in `production` it returns a plain text/HTML error page (NOT the consistent `{error:"message"}` JSON shape used everywhere else in the project). Known/expected errors are already handled with their own `res.status(...).json({error:...})` in these routes (which is good), but if a GENUINELY unexpected error occurs (e.g. a bug, an `undefined.property` access), the player gets an inconsistent/potentially info-leaking response. **Proposal:** generalize the error middleware in `server.js` — add a general fallback after the CORS special case (`res.status(500).json({error:"Server error."})`, log the error to the server log with `console.error` but don't send stack/detail to the client), also adding `NODE_ENV=production` to `render.yaml` would be an additional defensive layer. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** a general error middleware was added right after the CORS special-case middleware in `server.js` — it logs the full error to the server log with `console.error`, returning only `{"error":"Server error."}` (500) to the client, with no stack/detail leaking. `NODE_ENV=production` was added to the backend service's `envVars` in `render.yaml` (the extra defensive layer the PM proposed). Backend tests showed no regression (267/267) — since this middleware-level change (for the same reason as idea #23/#34, route tests only use the routers via `buildApp()`, never importing `server.js`'s middleware chain) couldn't be automatically tested, it was verified live with curl.

**Live verification (as the PM requested — deliberately throwing a temporary error in a route to see the correct JSON returned, then reverted):** I added a temporary `GET /api/__qa_throw__` route to the local dev server with a synchronous `throw new Error(...)`. Calling it with curl: HTTP **500**, the body was exactly `{"error":"Server error."}` — no stack trace/error message detail leaked. Confirmed in the server log (`console.error`) that the full error ("Unexpected server error: Error: deliberate QA error...") was genuinely written (the logging side also works, just doesn't reach the client). After verification, the temporary route was fully reverted (confirmed `git diff backend/server.js` shows only the real fix, `git status` shows no extra file) — never entered a commit, the endpoint returned 404 again.

55. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** There's no Error Boundary at all on the React side — an unexpected render error takes the entire application down to a blank white screen, the user sees no explanation.** There are zero results for `ErrorBoundary`/`componentDidCatch`/`getDerivedStateFromError` project-wide in `frontend/src` (confirmed via grep) — `App.tsx` is rendered directly in `main.tsx`, with no error boundary at all. While all API errors have been properly handled so far (via the `catch`/`error` state pattern, including idea #37), if a component throws an unexpected exception during the RENDER phase (e.g. an unexpectedly `null`/`undefined` field comes from the backend, or a future change misses an edge case), React's default behavior is to unmount the ENTIRE tree — the user is silently left with a completely blank page, without even a "refresh the page" suggestion, only an error visible in the console (which a normal player never opens). A standard robustness measure is missing for a "user-facing" prod application. **Proposal:** add a simple `ErrorBoundary` class component (`componentDidCatch`/`getDerivedStateFromError`), wrap `App` with it and show a fallback UI ("Something went wrong, refresh the page" + a refresh button) — a layer different from the `NetworkError`/fetch retry pattern (idea #4), catching render-time errors. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** the new `frontend/src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` — a `Component` class that catches the error via `getDerivedStateFromError`/`componentDidCatch` and shows a fallback using the `.game-over-screen` style (the existing ornate panel pattern) ("Something went wrong" + a "refreshing the page should fix it" message + a "Reload Page" button, `window.location.reload()`). `<App/>` in `main.tsx` was wrapped with this boundary. A new unit test file (`ErrorBoundary.test.tsx`, 3 tests): confirms children render as-is on an error-free render, that a child component throwing an error switches to the fallback UI (original content disappears), and that clicking "Reload Page" calls `window.location.reload()` (jsdom's read-only `location` was temporarily overridden with `vi.stubGlobal`). Frontend tests **83/83** (9 files, +1 new), `tsc -b` and `vite build` clean.

**Live verification (as the PM requested — deliberately throwing an error and confirming it's genuinely caught, then reverted):** I added a temporary, query-param-gated `throw new Error(...)` to `App.tsx` (`?__qa_throw__=1`), and tested against the local dev server with Playwright — the normal page (`http://localhost:5173/`) showed the character creation form, unaffected; the page opened with the error query param (`?__qa_throw__=1`) **showed the fallback UI** ("Something went wrong" + "Reload Page" button, also confirmed with a screenshot), the page was NOT blank/white (the body carried 130 characters of content), and no uncaught `pageerror` was thrown (React genuinely caught the error, only logging to the console in dev mode). After verification, the temporary throw code was fully reverted (confirmed via `git status` that no diff remained in `App.tsx`) — never entered a commit.

52. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, user decision: tie item use to Bonus Action — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** "Bonus Action" (`bonusActionAvailable`) is purely decorative, no mechanic is tied to it at all. `sceneFactory.js`/`scene.js`'s `advanceTurn()` sets `bonusActionAvailable = true` every turn, and it's shown as "Bonus: ✓/✗" in `TacticalGrid.tsx:297` (confirmed via grep — there's not a single route project-wide that SETS `bonusActionAvailable = false`, it's only set manually in test files). Meaning the indicator is always ✓, and no action (attack/spell/item use) consumes it — giving the player the impression of a resource that doesn't exist. Half of the BG3-inspired action economy design (see PROJECT.md/past phases) was never implemented. **Proposal (aligned with the real D&D 5e/BG3 convention):** tie item use (`/item/use`, which currently consumes a full Action) to Bonus Action — this both gives the indicator real meaning and makes it possible to both attack AND drink a potion in the same turn (in BG3, potion use is classically a bonus action). Since this is a balance change (strengthening the attack+heal combo in the same turn), not auto-assigning — a small/clear task if PM/user approves (a single-line change: `bonusActionAvailable=false` instead of `actionAvailable=false`, `requirePlayerAction` would need to be updated accordingly).

**User decision:** tie item use (item/use) to Bonus Action. **Fix:** `requireBonusAction()` was added to `scene.js` (shares the same turn-order/soft-lock (idea #35) logic as `requirePlayerAction`, just checks `bonusActionAvailable` instead of `actionAvailable`, error message "You have no Bonus Action left this turn."). `/item/use` now calls `requireBonusAction` and sets `playerToken.bonusActionAvailable = false` (never touches `actionAvailable`). `/item/throw` was deliberately NOT CHANGED — it still consumes a full Action (the PM's "you can optionally leave throw on a full Action" option was chosen, to keep the scope minimal and avoid an unwanted additional balance effect). Backend tests: 3 old tests (which assumed item/use consumed actionAvailable) updated for the new distinction + a new combo test added (attack+item/use both 200 in the same turn, a second item/use 400). Backend **267/267**.

**Live verification (real running dev server + real dice, NO mock):** I equipped a Fighter, waited for the goblin, and when adjacent, sent a real `/attack` (200, hit) — I then immediately drank a potion with `/item/use` in the SAME turn, and it **returned 200** (previously would have returned 400 since the Action was already spent — exactly the intended change). In the scene check, both `actionAvailable:false` (from attack) and `bonusActionAvailable:false` (from item/use) were correctly set. A third item-use attempt (same turn) was correctly rejected with `400 "You have no Bonus Action left this turn."`. Test character cleaned up with `/character/reset`.

51. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The "Action: ✗" indicator is only informational — clicking an adjacent enemy is still possible after the Action is exhausted, the backend just rejects it with 400.** `TacticalGrid.tsx:297` shows `playerToken.actionAvailable` as text (✓/✗), but the `gridInteractive` calculation (`specialMode ? true : isPlayerTurn`, confirmed via grep) never uses this value — it only checks "is it the player's turn." Meaning after the player has used their Action (attack/spell), the grid still appears fully interactive, an adjacent enemy can still be clicked, a request is sent, and the backend returns 400 with "You have no Action left this turn." (not silently lost, the `error` state already shows it — not a critical bug, but an unnecessary round-trip + a confusing "why can I click but it doesn't work" feeling). **Proposal:** add a `playerToken.actionAvailable` check to the `gridInteractive`/attack-click logic — if the Action is exhausted, enemy cells should still be clickable but appear disabled (a `grid-disabled`-style style), so the user understands why they can't click without going to the server. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** `actionExhausted` was derived in `TacticalGrid.tsx` (`isPlayerTurn && !specialMode && playerToken && !playerToken.actionAvailable`), and this check was added to the enemy branch of `handleCellClick` — if exhausted, `attackTarget()` is NEVER called, a direct inline error ("You have no Action left this turn.") is shown instead. Visual: enemy cells (`.cell.enemy-disabled`, only the cursor) and their tokens (Phase 11's separate overlay layer — `.token-marker.token-disabled`, opacity+grayscale) appear disabled; movement is unaffected (the Action economy only covers attack/spell/item use, not the ENTIRE grid, only enemies become disabled). Bonus: the existing `.throw-hint` text now reads "You have no Action left this turn - you cannot attack enemies, but you can still move." when the Action is exhausted. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), `tsc -b` clean.

**Live verification (real running server.js + real browser + real dice, NO mock):** I positioned a goblin adjacent to the player using the same technique as idea #45/#46/#47. The first click genuinely went to `/scene/attack` (Action consumed, `attackRequestCount:1`) — afterward `Action: ✗`, the hint text updated, and the goblin's token (`token-disabled`) and cell (`enemy-disabled`) visually faded (also confirmed with a screenshot). Clicking the SAME cell a SECOND time kept `attackRequestCount` **fixed at 1** (never went to the backend), and the inline "You have no Action left this turn." error was shown — the old unnecessary round-trip entirely eliminated. No console errors. Temporary server/files cleaned up, the real dev server restarted with normal settings, backend **266/266** stable (this item never touched the backend).

50. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** Weapons (hand slot) show no icon at all in the inventory list — neither an image nor a fallback emoji, completely blank.** A finding the coder noticed during idea #49 (see the note below), confirmed: `SLOT_ICONS.hand = null` in `backend/data/itemSlots.js` (weapons have no asset at all, a deliberate design — see the comment at the top of the file), meaning `item.icon` is always `null` for weapons. The render logic at `CharacterCard.tsx:346-349` is `item.icon ? <img/> : (!item.slot && <fallback emoji/>)` — since weapons' `item.slot` IS SET ("hand", not empty/null), `!item.slot` is false, so the fallback emoji doesn't render either. Result: Short Sword/Dagger x2/Staff/Mace carry no visual marker at all in the inventory list other than their name (the equipped paper-doll slot has an emoji via `SLOT_EMOJI.hand`, but not in the inventory LIST), breaking visual consistency with all other item categories (armor has an image, consumable/flavor items have an emoji since idea #49). **Proposal:** replace the `!item.slot` check with `!item.icon` (a more general/correct condition — "show fallback if there's no image", independent of slot), and show `SLOT_EMOJI.hand` (the ⚔-like emoji already used in the paper-doll) as the fallback for weapons too. A small/clear task.

**Fix:** instead of the PM's proposed `SLOT_EMOJI.hand`, the `SLOT_FALLBACK_GLYPH` map, already present in the project and defined exactly for this purpose, was used (its comment: "the tgstation icon set has no weapon icon - emoji fallback for the hand slot" — the paper-doll also uses the same map, `SLOT_EMOJI` serves a different purpose, the icon of the slot HEADER). A new `getInventoryFallbackIcon(item)` helper: returns `SLOT_FALLBACK_GLYPH[slot]` if `item.slot` exists (null otherwise), otherwise `getConsumableIcon(item.name)` (idea #49). The render condition in the inventory list changed from `item.icon ? <img/> : (!item.slot && fallback)` to `item.icon ? <img/> : (show fallback if it exists)` — the PM's proposed "show fallback if there's no image" logic. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), `tsc -b` clean.

47. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, own test/QA — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The third/final missing piece of the damage visual-reaction family (idea #39, #46) — no shake/flash/popup at all when an enemy hits the player (during End Turn).** `handleEndTurn()` at `TacticalGrid.tsx:222-226` — calls the `endTurn()` API and updates the scene, but never calls `triggerDamageFx` (confirmed via grep). #39 covered the player's `/attack`, #46 the player's `/cast` (including AoE), but the moment an enemy hits the player (`/scene/end-turn`, `runEnemyTurn()`) still has zero visual reaction — only the narration text ("you took X damage") and the HP bar (idea #39) silently shrinking. **Note:** the `/end-turn` backend does NOT RETURN a structured damage number (only free text `enemyMessages`) — the simplest approach, without touching the backend, could be to compare `character.hp.current` BEFORE and AFTER the `endTurn()` call and show the difference via `triggerDamageFx` at the player token's coordinates (App.tsx already refetches the character in `handleTurnResolved`, which could be passed as a prop to `TacticalGrid`, or `TacticalGrid` could watch its own `playerHp` prop's change with a `useEffect` and trigger it). A small-medium task, can be picked up once the coder is free.

**Fix:** `handleEndTurn()` — the approach the PM proposed (without touching the backend): the `playerHp` prop value is captured BEFORE the turn starts, and after the `endTurn()` response is processed, if `enemyMessages.length>0` (the enemy genuinely did something), a fresh `getCurrentCharacter()` request fetches the CURRENT HP and computes the difference; if the difference is >0, `triggerDamageFx` (the same mechanism converted to an array in idea #46) is triggered at the player token's coordinate (current in `updated.tokens` since it didn't move). On error (network, etc.) it's silently swallowed — this is only a visual polish, it shouldn't block the main turn flow. A direct fetch was preferred over `useEffect`-based prop-watching (App.tsx's OWN `handleTurnResolved` also separately fetches the same `/character` — there are two parallel requests, but the endpoint is rate-limit-free/cheap, simpler than extra state synchronization complexity). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80, this feature had no dedicated test before either), `tsc -b` clean.

**Live verification (real running server.js + real browser + real dice, NO mock):** using the same technique as idea #45/#46, I started the real server on a separate port and positioned a goblin adjacent to the player, then pressed "End Turn." The real dice hit on the FIRST attempt: the chat showed "The Goblin lands a solid hit! You took 3 damage. (HP: 9/12)", and I immediately checked the DOM with Playwright — the player's cell (1,1) had BOTH the `.damage-flash` class AND the `.damage-popup` span present. No console errors. Temporary server/files cleaned up, the real dev server restarted with normal settings, backend **266/266** stable (this item never touched the backend).

43. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder, user decision: AoE — also took on the PM+tester role in the same session)** The "Fireball" spell isn't AoE (area-effect), it's actually an ordinary single-target attack. `backend/data/spells.js` — `fireball: {type:"attack", range:3, damageDie:8}`, D&D's defining feature of Fireball (hitting a point and damaging ALL enemies in its radius) was never implemented — the `spell.type==="attack"` branch of `/cast` in `backend/routes/scene.js` (confirmed via grep) processes a single `targetTokenId` with logic identical to regular `/attack` (single target, single damage). Meaning in its current form, Fireball is no different from a mana-consuming ranged sword strike — mechanically there's no "area" feel at all. This could especially matter in multi-enemy encounters like Skeleton Graveyard (2 skeletons). **Why I didn't auto-assign this:** it requires a genuine balance decision — what should the blast radius be (adjacent to the target, a fixed range), is the player affected if also within the radius, how is XP distributed on a multi-kill (separately per enemy or all at once) — not a small code change, a decision that genuinely changes game balance. PM/user should prioritize, not auto-assigned.

**User decision:** implement AoE. **Fix:** `backend/routes/scene.js` — a new `FIREBALL_AOE_RADIUS=1` constant was added. `/cast`'s `spell.type==="attack"` branch: after the hit against the target is determined (a single D20 vs DC12 check, if it misses no enemy is affected), the target itself AND ALL other enemies (`scene.tokens.filter(t=>t.type==="enemy" && ...)`) within Manhattan distance ≤1 of it are included in the blast — a SEPARATE damage roll is made for each (so even within the same blast they can take different damage by chance). `awardXp()` is called SEPARATELY for each enemy killed (total XP correctly accumulates on a multi-kill, multiple level-ups are also possible). The player is never included in this radius (the filter is already `type==="enemy"`, no PvE/friendly fire — no code change needed, a natural consequence). A "The blast hit N enemies, M were defeated!" sentence was added to the narration (when more than one enemy is hit; the old "{name} was defeated!" sentence was preserved for a single target, backward-compatible). A new `blastHits` field was added to the response (`[{id,name,damage,defeated}]`, for debug/QA) — the existing `damage`/`defeated` fields still refer ONLY to the actually-clicked target (the frontend never used these fields anyway, no type breakage). `checkEncounterCleared` is now called with a `defeatedHits.length>0` check (previously only if the primary target died) - killing the last two enemies AT ONCE with AoE also correctly triggers clearing the encounter.

**Test:** 3 new tests were added to `scene.test.js` (with a deterministic `Math.random` mock) — (a) a second enemy adjacent to the target also takes damage and dies + a third enemy OUTSIDE the blast radius is entirely unaffected + total 40 XP for 2 kills + narration text correct, (b) the player's HP never changes even if within the blast radius (adjacent to the target), (c) on a miss, no one takes damage, including the adjacent enemy. Backend tests **266/266** (from 263 to 266, all new).

**Live verification (real Express app + real dice/RNG, NO mock):** I tried playing through the early encounters (goblin/spider) to reach Skeleton Graveyard via the live server, but low-HP classes (Wizard/Cleric) died within a few RNG-dependent attempts (not a genuine balance finding, just a weakness in my own automated play strategy) — for time efficiency, I directly set up the Skeleton Graveyard scene with real `sceneFactory.createScene(2)` and positioned two skeletons adjacent to each other (the same legitimate test-setup pattern as `characters.get(id).hp.current=X` in the backend test files, but against a real running Express route, with real dice, Math.random NOT MOCKED). Result: after a miss + a critical miss, a hit dealt DIFFERENT damage to the two skeletons (3 and 2, confirming separate rolls), and with continued real rolls, on the fourth cast BOTH died AT ONCE (`defeated:true` for both), XP was exactly **40**, the narration "The blast hit 2 enemies, 2 were defeated! You've cleared the area, moving on..." was generated correctly (the encounter-cleared transition also correctly triggered), and the player's HP (10/10) never changed throughout. Temporary test files deleted, the real dev server's rate-limit restored to normal.

Meaning a player can't see how much damage their enemy has taken/how much HP is left, only getting a binary "did it die or not" signal (once the token disappears) — not an issue for weak enemies dying in 1-2 melee hits, but with Phase 10's armor/weapon mechanic (you may need to hit the same enemy 3-4 times), a tactical information gap is felt, and additionally, on mobile/touch the `title` tooltip practically never works at all (no hover). **Proposal:** add a small mini HP bar below/above `.token-marker` (consistent with the existing bar pattern in `CharacterCard`, 3-4px height is enough) — for both player and enemy tokens, `token.hp`/`token.maxHp` is already present in `scene.tokens` (the backend doesn't need to return anything extra). A small/clear task.

**Fix:** a 3px-tall `.token-hp-bar`/`.token-hp-bar-fill` was added to the `.token-marker` overlay in `TacticalGrid.tsx` (`App.css`, the same visual language as the HP/mana bars), with the color changing to green→orange(`.mid`, ≤50%)→red(`.low`, ≤25%) based on fill ratio. **An unexpected finding (caught in my own QA):** the player token in `scene.tokens` never carried an `hp`/`maxHp` field at all (the backend's `sceneFactory.js` only adds `hp`/`maxHp` to enemies, player HP is kept in `character.hp`) — to satisfy the idea's "both player and enemy" request, I passed `App.tsx`'s already-available `character.hp.current`/`character.hp.max` down to `TacticalGrid` via new `playerHp`/`playerMaxHp` props, used for the token with `id==='player'` (the backend was not touched). Verified live with Playwright (local dev server): in a fresh encounter, a full green bar appeared on both the player and Goblin tokens; I ran a real 8-turn fight, dropping both sides' HP — the bars correctly updated width/color at every step (Goblin turned `.low` red at 10→2 HP, the player likewise at 12→1 HP), also confirmed with a screenshot (exactly consistent with the "(HP: 1/12)"/"(HP: 1/12)" messages in the chat log). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), `tsc -b` clean.

38. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder)** There's no XP/level progress bar anywhere during gameplay — the player doesn't know how far they are from the next level, only seeing XP for the first time on the Game Over screen (after dying). `CharacterCard.tsx:194` only shows `Level {character.level}`, `character.xp` is never rendered (confirmed via grep — `character.xp` only appears passed as a prop to `GameOverScreen` in `App.tsx:231`, nowhere in the game screen). The backend already makes this easy: `awardXp()` in `backend/services/leveling.js` subtracts `xpToNextLevel(level)` from `character.xp` on every level-up (line 23) — meaning `character.xp` already holds progress WITHIN the current level, no extra backend calculation is needed for the bar, only the `xpToNextLevel(level) = level*50` formula (or `xpToNextLevel` is already exported via `module.exports`) needs to be known on the frontend. **Fix:** a new "⭐ XP" `bar-row` was added to `CharacterCard.tsx` between the HP and Mana bars — the exact same visual pattern as the existing HP/mana bars (`.bar`/`.bar-fill`), with a new gold-colored `.bar-fill.xp` (`var(--gold)`). The `xpToNextLevel(level)=level*50` and `MAX_LEVEL=20` constants from `backend/services/leveling.js` were exactly repeated on the frontend (no code sharing in the project, since it's a simple formula/constant, not deemed necessary to also fetch from the API). When `character.level >= MAX_LEVEL`, the bar shows 100% full with the text "MAX", otherwise `{xp}/{xpToNextLevel(level)}` + fill ratio. Verified live with Playwright: a fresh character shows "0/50" (an empty bar); killing a real goblin and `awardXp()` genuinely giving +20 XP updates the bar to "20/50"; I also verified the MAX_LEVEL state by directly patching the DB (`level:20,xp:0`) and restarting the backend — the bar text shows "MAX". Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

**[2026-08-25] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38) — idea #38 against real prod, with the scenario the PM proposed:** I created a fresh character and opened the panel — the XP bar correctly showed "0/50" (`bar-fill.xp` `style.width: 0%`). I equipped gear and killed a real goblin via the API (`awardXp` gave +20 XP, confirmed in the API), refreshed the page and reopened the panel — the bar correctly updated to "20/50" and `style.width: 40%` (20/50=40% math correct). A screenshot also confirmed it was visually clean (a gold-colored bar, the same pattern as HP/Mana, no overflow/alignment issue — even rendering correctly with the breathing-room banner and the panel open at the same time). I didn't re-test the MAX_LEVEL state since patching prod's DB is risky/unnecessary (the coder already verified it locally). Test character cleaned up. **Result: idea #38 works correctly, no bug.**

37. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder)** If an error occurs while sending a chat message, the message silently disappears — `ChatPanel.tsx` has no `catch` at all. `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel.tsx:42-54` — `handleSend()`: the box is cleared FIRST via `setInput('')`, then `await sendChatMessage(text)` inside `try{...} finally{setSending(false)}` — **there's no `catch` block at all**. If `sendChatMessage` is rejected (a backend error/400, `NetworkError`/cold-start, OR the 429 rate-limit just added in idea #36 — which affects exactly this path), the message is never added to the `messages` list (that line is never reached), and since the input was already cleared, the user LOSES WHAT THEY WROTE, with no error/feedback shown on screen either — a silent "as if nothing happened" behavior (other than an unhandled promise rejection in the console). In other flows (like the `NetworkError`/retry pattern in `App.tsx`), similar errors are at least communicated to the user with a message — `ChatPanel` never follows this pattern. **Fix:** an `error` state was added to `ChatPanel.tsx`, and `catch (err) { setError((err as Error).message); }` was added to `handleSend`. `setInput('')` is now called AFTER `sendChatMessage` SUCCEEDS, not BEFORE the request starts — on error, the user's typed text stays in the input as-is (no need to restore it, it's never cleared). The error message (`err.message` — the user-friendly text the backend returns, e.g. #36's rate-limit message or a general network error) is shown below the message list with the same `<p className="error">` pattern used in `TacticalGrid`. Verified live by triggering a real 429 (Playwright, within a single script: exhausting the same IP's shared 20-request budget via `/character/create`+19×`/chat`, then immediately sending a message via the real UI) — upon clicking send, the text in the input ("This message shouldn't disappear!") stayed EXACTLY as-is, "Too many requests sent, please wait a bit and try again." appeared on screen, and the message was not added to the list (no silent disappearance). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

**[2026-08-25] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38) — idea #37 against real prod, repeating the same scenario as the coder (triggering a real 429):** I created a fresh character, exhausted the same IP's shared 20-request budget (`/character/create` + 19× `/character/roll-stats`), then sent a message ("This message shouldn't disappear") via the REAL browser UI — upon clicking send: the text in the input stayed EXACTLY as-is (confirmed via `inputValue()`), "Too many requests sent, please wait a bit and try again." correctly appeared on screen, the `.chat-message` count stayed at 1 (only the opening message — the failed send was NEVER added to the list, no silent disappearance), the send button didn't stay locked (can be retried). A screenshot also confirmed it was visually clean (error text above the input, layout not broken). Test character cleaned up. **Result: idea #37 works exactly as claimed, no bug.**

36. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder)** `POST /api/chat` has no rate-limit at all — a single script could exhaust the restricted AI budget within seconds. Idea #2 (closed in Phase 9) only added `publicRateLimit` to `/character/create` and `/character/roll-stats` — `backend/routes/chat.js` (confirmed via grep, no `publicRateLimit` import/usage at all) is unauthenticated-accessible and still entirely unlimited. This is especially serious because every `/chat` request attempts an AI call via `generateNarration`→ consuming the shared/global `AI_HOURLY_LIMIT=30` budget in `render.yaml` (`services/rateLimiter.js`'s `allowRequest()` is hourly-LIMITED and shared across ALL players) — a single malicious or just fast-clicking user could exhaust the ENTIRE 30-request hourly AI budget within seconds, dropping EVERYONE to mock for the rest of that hour (this could also be another possible root cause of idea #19's "AI always falls back to mock" complaint). Additionally, every request writes to the DB via `saveChatHistory` — the spam also generates storage/CPU cost. **Fix:** `publicRateLimit` was added to `chat.js` in the same pattern as `character.js` (`router.post("/", publicRateLimit, async (req,res)=>{...`, one line). The `/scene/*` endpoints were deliberately not touched (this idea's scope was `/chat` only, the most critical/cheapest win). Backend tests showed no regression (263/263 — `publicRateLimit` already a no-op under `VITEST`). Verified live with a real session: 23 `/chat` requests were sent in a row from the same IP (+1 earlier `/character/create`) — the first 19 requests returned 201, and from the 20th request on (once exactly 20 requests total filled up), it consistently returned 429 + `RateLimit-*`/`Retry-After` headers. Since the limit is `publicRateLimit`'s SINGLE module-level middleware instance, it's a budget SHARED across all public endpoints per IP (create+roll-stats+chat) — this even more thoroughly blocks exactly the scenario the idea mentioned ("a single fast script exhausts the hourly AI budget"). Normal/slow usage (well under 20 requests/minute) is unaffected.

**[2026-08-25] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38) — idea #36 against real prod:** I created a fresh character (1 request) and sent 2 normal chat messages — all three worked fine (201). I then sent 22 more `/chat` requests in a row to the same session — the first 17 got 201, and from the 18th on, consistent `429 {"error":"Too many requests sent, please wait a bit and try again."}` (filled up at exactly 20 requests total — exactly matching the coder's number). The shared counter was also confirmed: after the limit filled up, I tried `/character/roll-stats` with a DIFFERENT session-id, and it also returned 429 (confirmed to work IP-based, independent of session). `GET /api/chat` (reading message history) never hit the rate-limit, only `POST` is affected. Test character cleaned up. **Result: idea #36 works correctly, no bug.**

35. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder) — a genuine soft-lock.** Using/throwing an item outside combat (in text mode) gets stuck on the "Action" economy — the "End Turn" button, the only way to reset it, exists ONLY in the combat grid. `backend/routes/scene.js:290-301` — `requirePlayerAction()` is called in `/item/use` (line 515) and `/item/throw` (line 598); it rejects the request if `scene.activeTokenId !== "player"` OR `playerToken.actionAvailable === false`. `actionAvailable` is reset to `true` only by `advanceTurn()` (line 247, triggered from the `/end-turn` route). But on the frontend, the "End Turn" button renders ONLY inside `TacticalGrid.tsx` (`frontend/src/App.tsx:242,319` — `showGrid`/`mode-combat`, i.e. while there's an enemy) (confirmed via grep, `endTurn()` is called nowhere else). Result: if a player drinks a potion or throws an item outside combat (in Phase 11's text-first exploration mode, with no enemy), `actionAvailable` becomes `false`/`activeTokenId` may not be "player", and it's NEVER reset again — since the grid isn't visible, there's nowhere to press "End Turn", and the player becomes unable to use/throw any item until entering the next battle (until the grid opens and End Turn becomes accessible) (a soft-lock). In D&D, the "Action" concept is only meaningful within a combat turn anyway — drinking a potion during exploration consuming an "action" has no game-logic justification either. **Fix:** `requirePlayerAction()` now, when there's no enemy at all in the scene (`scene.tokens.some(t=>t.type==="enemy")` false), entirely skips the turn/Action check ("It's not your turn."/"You have no Action left this turn.") and directly returns `playerToken` — since this function is shared by `/attack`, `/cast`, `/item/use`, `/item/throw`, it was fixed from a single point (attack/cast already return their own 404s for finding no target in an enemy-free scene, behavior unchanged; as a bonus, Heal is also now free outside combat — another manifestation of the same soft-lock). A new backend test was added: confirming that when the scene has no enemies and `actionAvailable=false`, two consecutive `/item/use` calls BOTH return 200 (previously the second one would be rejected with 400). An existing Phase 7-A test ("attacking an already-defeated enemy again") was updated — now, once the last enemy dies (entering the breather window), a second attack is rejected with 404 because the target doesn't exist in the scene at all, not "no action left" (400) (a reversion to the original Phase 7-A behavior from before idea #35, makes sense). Verified live with a real scene (via script): a real goblin was killed and the breather window entered (`pendingEncounterIndex:1`, no enemy, `actionAvailable` happened to already be `true` but that doesn't matter since we're bypassing the check), and two consecutive `/item/use` calls BOTH returned 200 — the old code would reject the second call with 400. Backend tests showed no regression (263/263, 1 new test added).

**[2026-08-25] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38) — idea #35 against real prod, with the scenario the PM proposed (kill a real enemy → 2 consecutive actions in the breather window):** I killed a real goblin with a Cleric (entering breathing room with `pendingEncounterIndex:1`), then called `/scene/cast` (Heal) twice in a row WITHOUT ever calling `/end-turn` — **both returned 200** (HP 7/10→10/10 correctly capped, mana 10→2 correctly dropped, 2×4). Even though the scene response showed `actionAvailable:false`, the second call also succeeded — confirming the fix's "skip the check entirely if there's no enemy" logic works exactly as claimed. (Note: on the first attempt, while trying to pick up a 2nd potion, I accidentally advanced to the next encounter — `move`+`end-turn` automatically advances while in the breather window, expected behavior, I switched my test strategy to cast.) Test character cleaned up with `/character/reset`. **Result: the soft-lock is fully resolved, no bug.**

34. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder)** Backend CORS is entirely open with no origin restriction at all (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`). `backend/server.js:34` — `app.use(cors())` is called with no arguments, and the `cors` package's default is to allow all origins. `helmet()` (idea #23) and CSP (idea #24) closed off standard hardening between frontend/backend, but the backend ITSELF still allows any third-party site (e.g. a malicious page) to directly send a `fetch()` request to this API from the browser — since the `X-Session-Id` header isn't automatically sent on simple requests, classic CSRF risk is low, but a malicious page could generate its own `X-Session-Id` and call the API through the victim's IP/browser (consuming rate-limit on the victim's browser's behalf, or generating illegitimate automated character/spam traffic) — meaning an unrestricted origin makes exploitation of "unauthenticated-accessible endpoints" (like `/character/create`, which idea #2 mentions) easier. **Fix:** `backend/server.js` — `cors()` is no longer called with no arguments, it's called with an `origin` function: the allowed origin list is a union of fixed local dev origins (`http://localhost:5173`, `http://127.0.0.1:5173`) + those coming (comma-separated) from the `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` env variable. Requests WITHOUT an Origin header (curl, supertest, server-to-server calls, health checks) always pass — the `cors` package's standard behavior, deliberate so as not to break non-browser clients. A request from a disallowed origin is given as an error to `cors`'s callback, caught by a new error-handling middleware that returns 403 + a clean JSON error message (instead of the default Express error page). A fixed `FRONTEND_ORIGIN=https://dnd-game-frontend-t9hr.onrender.com` value was added to `render.yaml` (the same pattern as `VITE_API_BASE` — not a secret, no need to set anything manually in the dashboard). Verified live with curl: `Origin: http://localhost:5173` (both a simple request AND preflight) correctly returns the `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header; `Origin: http://evil.example.com` returns 403 + an error message on both a simple request and preflight, with NO `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header leaking at all; a plain curl with no Origin header returns a normal 200. Verified with Playwright that a real browser flow (in dev, Vite's proxy routes `/api` to the same origin so CORS doesn't kick in anyway, but as a regression check) — character creation still works fine. Backend tests showed no regression (262/262 — route-level tests never import `server.js` (and hence the cors/helmet middleware) via `buildApp()`, only using the routers; this middleware-level change was verified live with curl using the same pattern as helmet in #23, instead of a unit test).

**[2026-08-25] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38) — idea #34 (CORS allowlist) against real prod, tested especially carefully since this change affects ALL frontend↔backend traffic:** I tested a legitimate origin (`https://dnd-game-frontend-t9hr.onrender.com`), a foreign origin (`http://evil.example.com`), a request without an origin header, and the local dev origin (`http://localhost:5173`) separately with curl. **A transient finding:** on the first round, the legitimate prod origin UNEXPECTEDLY got 403 on a few requests (I must have hit a rolling-deploy moment where the deploy hadn't yet propagated to all Render instances — old/new code was running simultaneously on different instances) — confirmed with 10 consecutive requests, it fully stabilized within a few minutes (legitimate origin consistently 200, foreign origin consistently 403, origin-less/localhost always 200). I also verified a full end-to-end character creation flow with a real browser (Playwright) — no CORS blocking, completed without issue. **Result: idea #34 works correctly, no permanent bug** — just noting the transient inconsistency at deploy time (not a code bug, Render's rolling-deploy window; in the future, waiting a few minutes rather than testing immediately after deploying a similar security-critical middleware change would be more reliable).
