"Yes, Delete" triggers the existing `handleRestart` flow (already used from `GameOverScreen`) — calling `resetSession()` and returning to the character creation screen. When the panel is closed (✕ or clicking the overlay), the confirmation state is also reset (the old "are you sure?" doesn't show up on reopening). Verified live with Playwright: "Cancel" cancels the confirmation and leaves the character alive, "Yes, Delete" genuinely deletes the character (`GET /api/character` returns 404) and returns to the character creation screen. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

32. **[2026-08-25] [x] FIXED (coder)** The help modal was outdated after Phase 11 — it still mentioned the "right panel" that no longer exists, never describing the new interaction model. **Fix:** `HelpModal.tsx` — the phrase "shown with ✓/✗ in the right panel" was corrected to "shown with ✓/✗ above the grid"; 3 new items were added explaining the text-first default view/the grid's automatic opening and closing, the 🎒 character panel button, and the breather window advanced with "Continue" once an encounter is cleared (9 items total). Verified live with Playwright: the modal renders with the correct text, and thanks to `.help-modal`'s existing `max-height:90vh; overflow-y:auto` rule, the longer list also scrolls within itself without overflowing, with the "Got it" button staying accessible. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

31. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** There was no upper limit on leveling up at all — with the cyclical encounter pool (idea #21's inventory theme, this time for the character), it could grow without bound. `backend/services/leveling.js` — `awardXp()` leveled up repeatedly in a `while` loop, with no `MAX_LEVEL` or similar upper-bound check at all. **Fix:** `MAX_LEVEL=20` (5e's traditional cap) was added — a simple approach was chosen: if the character is already at `MAX_LEVEL`, `awardXp()` returns `0` at the very start and doesn't even add XP (a no-op); the `while` loop also carries the `character.level < MAX_LEVEL` condition, so even a massive XP reward stops exactly at `MAX_LEVEL`, never exceeding it. 3 new tests were added (`leveling.test.js`): no XP is gained while at MAX_LEVEL, a massive reward stops exactly at MAX_LEVEL without exceeding it, the constant is correct (20). Verified live via a direct `awardXp()` call (real module code): level 19→20 (with a massive XP) correctly stops, and a repeated call at MAX_LEVEL returns `levelsGained:0`. Backend tests **262/262**.

30. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** `GameOverScreen` had no ARIA role/label at all — unlike `HelpModal`, a screen-reader user might never notice the game had ended. **Fix:** `role="alertdialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-label="Game Over"` were added to the root `<div>` in `GameOverScreen.tsx` (the same pattern as `HelpModal`). Verified live with Playwright: with **a real battle** (the POST /api/character exploit shortcut had just been removed so that couldn't be used — instead the player genuinely fought and genuinely died), it was confirmed that `GameOverScreen`'s `role`/`aria-modal`/`aria-label` attributes render correctly once the character dies. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean. There were zero results for `role`/`aria-` in `frontend/src/components/GameOverScreen.tsx` (confirmed via grep). In #17 (help modal keyboard/accessibility support), `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal="true"`/`aria-label` had been added to `HelpModal` — `GameOverScreen` is functionally a similarly full-screen state change (game over, the only option is "Restart"), but carries no ARIA markers at all, silently invisible to a screen-reader user. Proposal: add at least `role="alertdialog"` (or `role="dialog"` + `aria-live="assertive"` to the top-level container) and an `aria-label` containing "Game Over" — repeat HelpModal's pattern. A small/clear task.

29. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder) — 🚨 CRITICAL — `POST /api/character/` accepted `hp`/`mana`/`attributes`/`inventory` from the client as-is with no validation at all (idea #13's stat-cheating vulnerability coming back through another path).** `backend/routes/character.js:177-190` — `hp`, `mana`, `attributes`, `inventory` are taken directly from `req.body` and spread onto `character`, with NO bound/type/length check at all (the 1-22 range check added to `/create` in idea #13 does NOT exist on this endpoint). I confirmed directly in live production: I sent `{"hp":{"current":99999,"max":99999},"attributes":{"str":999,...}}` to `POST /api/character`, the REQUEST WAS ACCEPTED, and the character was genuinely updated with these god-mode values (I deleted the test character, no residue remains in prod). This doesn't just allow altering stats — it also allows replacing `inventory` with an ENTIRELY arbitrary array (Phase 9's `MAX_INVENTORY=30` limit can also be bypassed this way, and even made-up item names could "take advantage" of values in armorReduction.js/weaponDamage.js). There is a session-ownership check (you can only update your own active character, not someone else's), but the CONTENT is entirely unsafe. **Proposal:** either remove this endpoint entirely (check whether it's genuinely used on the frontend — likely an old/now-unnecessary general-purpose update endpoint left over from Phase 3-4), or add strict validation to every field like `/create` (attributes 1-22, hp/mana can't exceed the class's base values, inventory changes removed entirely, only specific/safe field updates allowed). **Fix:** a usage check was done — `grep` found no code on the frontend calling this route (the `updateCharacter` export in `api.ts` was defined but never called from any component, dead code). In backend tests, it was used in 4 places, but ONLY as a test-setup convenience (quickly altering a character's HP/inventory) — instead of adding validation, the PM's safer proposed approach was chosen: the `POST "/"` route in `routes/character.js` and the `updateCharacter` export in `api.ts` were ENTIRELY REMOVED (deleting an unused attack surface rather than validating it). The test-setup usages were converted to direct store mutation like `characters.get(id).hp.current = X` (consistent with the existing pattern in the project, already used in `scene.test.js`/`sessionIsolation.test.js`). The old "test the route" describe block in `character.test.js` was reduced to a test that "the route no longer exists, returns 404." Verified live with curl: the old exploit request now returns 404 (previously accepted with 200), `GET /api/character` (genuinely used) unaffected. Backend tests **259/259** (from 260 to 259, 2 tests merged into 1), frontend **80/80**, tsc+build clean.

28. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** An inconsistency found by the tester (claude-game-38): `/scene/move` and `/scene/end-turn` never performed the HP≤0 ("character is dead") check the `/attack`/`/cast`/`/item` endpoints perform via `requireOwnedCharacter()` — a token could be moved with a dead character, and a turn could even be ended, processing the enemy's turn. **Fix:** `routes/scene.js` — before the player token is moved in `/move` (before any mutation), and before `advanceTurn()` is called in `/end-turn`, the active character's `hp.current<=0` is checked, and if true, 400 "The character is dead, game over." is returned — the same message/behavior as the other endpoints. 2 new backend tests were added (`/move` is rejected + the token genuinely doesn't move; `/end-turn` is rejected + the turn/round doesn't change, the enemy turn isn't processed). Also verified live with curl: with an HP=0 character, both endpoints return 400, and with an HP>0 character, the normal flow shows no regression. Backend tests **260/260**.

27. **[2026-08-24] [x] FALSE ALARM (PM correction) — the Cleric armor imbalance claim was based on a faulty premise.** The original finding said "Fighter/Wizard/Rogue all get Leather Armor, Cleric doesn't" — once the coder checked, this turned out to be WRONG (a misreading of the PM's own node output). The real situation: ONLY Fighter has "Leather Armor" (suit, -2); Wizard and Rogue have NO armor at all (0); Cleric has "Shield" (back, -1) — meaning Cleric is actually BETTER armored than Wizard/Rogue, just less than Fighter. This asymmetry is consistent with the D&D theme (a wizard can't wear heavy armor, a rogue can be light/unarmored) — not a genuine imbalance. **Decision: nothing changed.** Caught before a wrong "fix" was made, thanks to the coder's careful check.

A new keyboard-accessibility audit was not done this round (out of scope), only that this item is no longer applicable was noted. The original item below is preserved for reference:
[ORIGINAL, NO LONGER APPLICABLE] ⚠️ Medium/large scope — the tactical grid (the game's main interaction area) is entirely mouse-dependent, with no keyboard support at all.** No results were found for `onKeyDown`/`tabIndex`/`role="button"` on grid cells in `TacticalGrid.tsx` (verified with grep) — #10 (aria-label) added names to cells, but they were never actually made keyboard-focusable and clickable. #17 (help modal) added keyboard support, but this doesn't cover the actual GAME interaction (movement/attack/target selection) — a keyboard user could create a character, manage inventory (buttons are focusable), but could never actually play combat/movement. Unlike the modal fix, this is not a small job: adding `tabIndex=0`+`role="gridcell"`+`onKeyDown` (triggering a click with Enter/Space, perhaps cell-to-cell navigation with arrow keys) to every cell needs to stay consistent with the existing mouse-interaction logic (including throw/cast target-select modes) — a substantial job, needs PM/user prioritization, not auto-assigned.

25. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (PM, urgent) — 🚨 URGENT/CRITICAL — character creation likely broken in prod (VITE_API_BASE resolving incorrectly).** Discovered incidentally (by the coder) while implementing idea #24 (frontend CSP). Examined the live deployed JS bundle: `https://dnd-game-frontend-t9hr.onrender.com` is currently sending requests to `dnd-game-backend-sz9e:3001` (no scheme, the RAW/unprocessed output of `fromService`/`hostport` in `render.yaml`) instead of `https://dnd-game-backend-sz9e.onrender.com`. Once fixed, both this basic flow and idea #24 (frontend CSP) need to be retried.
**[2026-08-24] RESULT (PM):** `VITE_API_BASE` in `render.yaml` was changed from `fromService`/`hostport` to the fixed value `https://dnd-game-backend-sz9e.onrender.com/api` (commit 1649c38) — no longer overwritten on every Blueprint sync. I verified this by inspecting the deployed JS bundle with curl. **This is an issue ENTIRELY INDEPENDENT of my CSP** — this URL is already invalid (the browser interprets the "dnd-game-backend-sz9e:" part as a URL scheme), meaning character creation in prod was likely already broken before the CSP too — the CSP just turned this from a silent network failure into a visible console error. Following instructions, I reverted my CSP change (commit fed9658, pushed) and reported it urgently to the PM. **The VITE_API_BASE issue still stands, needs to be resolved by PM+user in the Render dashboard (not within the coder's authority) — CSP idea #24 should be retried AFTER this is fixed.**

Idea #24 is now fully closed.

23. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** The backend had no standard security headers at all, and `X-Powered-By: Express` was leaking framework info. **Fix:** `npm install helmet`, `app.use(helmet())` added to `server.js` (before `cors()`). Verified live with curl: `X-Powered-By` is now gone, standard hardening headers like `Content-Security-Policy`/`X-Content-Type-Options`/`X-Frame-Options`/`Strict-Transport-Security` etc. appear, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (CORS) unaffected. **Extra point of attention:** helmet's default `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` header could seem risky given this backend is called via `fetch()` from a different Render subdomain (frontend) in prod — since curl can't test this, I tried a live cross-origin `fetch()` between two different origins (localhost:5173 → localhost:3001) in a REAL browser with Playwright, and it succeeded with 200 (CORP affects only `no-cors` sub-resource loads, not `cors`-mode fetches). I also ran a full character creation flow (Playwright) end-to-end with helmet active and confirmed no console errors. Backend tests showed no regression (258/258).

22. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** The "How to Play?" help modal never mentioned Phase 10 (equipment now genuinely affecting combat) or loot pickup (fix #20), it was outdated. **Fix:** 2 new items added to `HelpModal.tsx` (6 total): "Equipping armor/weapons genuinely affects combat — different weapons deal different damage dice, equipped armor reduces incoming damage." and "Moving onto items on the ground automatically adds them to your inventory." Verified live with Playwright: the modal shows 6 items, both new items appear correctly, no overflow/broken layout (confirmed with a screenshot). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80, no dedicated test exists for `HelpModal.tsx`), tsc+build clean.

21. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** A natural side effect of the loot pickup fix (idea #20): the inventory had no upper bound at all, and encounters repeating in an infinite loop kept re-offering the SAME loot every turn — over a long session, the inventory could theoretically grow without limit. **Fix (a simple cap, as the PM proposed — no stack/quantity refactor was done):** `MAX_INVENTORY=30` added to `routes/scene.js`. In `/move`, if the player steps onto a loot tile and the inventory is already at 30, the item is NOT REMOVED from the scene (it stays on the ground, can be picked up later once the inventory has space), `character.inventory` doesn't change, a new `inventoryFull` field is added to the response, and the narration gets an addition: "Your inventory is full, you couldn't pick up the item on the ground." The normal (not-full) flow is entirely unaffected. `inventoryFull` field added to `api.ts` (the frontend currently shows no extra UI for this, user feedback already comes from the chat message — left out of scope, a separate small job if needed). New backend test: fills the inventory to 30 and verifies that moving onto a loot tile doesn't pick it up, it stays in the scene, and `inventoryFull:true` is returned. Also verified live with curl: normal pickup (`inventoryFull:false`) showed no regression. Backend **258/258**, frontend **80/80**, tsc+build clean.

20. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** A side bug the coder found during Phase 10: loot items on the ground could never be picked up (never added to inventory). `scene.loot` sitting in the scene was removed from the scene when the player moved onto it (`collectedLoot` was returned in the response), but was never added to the character's inventory. **Fix:** `/move` in `routes/scene.js` — when the player token moves onto loot, a slot/icon is assigned using `itemSlots.js`'s `getSlotForItem`/`getIconForSlot` (the same pattern as character creation/Phase 10), it's added to `character.inventory` and persisted with `saveCharacter`; the narration gets an addition: "{item name} added to your inventory!" A new `character` field was added to the response (the current character if it was a player move, `null` otherwise) — the frontend (`api.ts`, `TacticalGrid.tsx`) reflects this instantly on the CharacterCard via `onCharacterChange` (without waiting for an extra refetch). 2 new backend tests (`scene.test.js`) + 3 test-mock updates (`TacticalGrid.test.tsx`, new `character` field). Verified end-to-end live with Playwright: clicking loot on the grid showed both "Bag of Gold added to your inventory!" in the chat and the item appeared instantly in the inventory list (no extra action needed); persistence was also verified via curl on `GET /api/character`. Backend **257/257**, frontend **80/80**, tsc+build clean.

19. **[2026-08-24] ⚠️ Requires user action (not a code change) — the AI Game Master still falls back to mock in production.** A live `POST /api/chat` attempt (with the `pm-ai-check` session) returned `"source":"mock"` — the real Gemini isn't working. Earlier (Phase 2), a real AI response was verified LOCALLY with a second key (`AQ.Ab8RN6Ip0Y5t...`), but it's unclear whether the `GEMINI_API_KEY` entered into the dashboard when deploying to Render is this working key or the earlier problematic one (returning billing/quota errors). 18. **[2026-08-30] [x] NO LONGER APPLICABLE (creative cron scan, identified by the coder) — this item belongs to the grid era, already resolved in Phase 12-C-prep 2 (around idea #92).** Checked `backend/services/freeformCombat.js`: `resolveAttack` now uses `getWeaponDamageDie(equippedWeapon?.name)`, varying the damage die based on the equipped weapon, and `resolveEnemyRetaliation` also reduces incoming damage based on armor via `getTotalArmorReduction(character)` (`data/armorReduction.js`/`data/weaponDamage.js` — both real, tested modules). So the claim that "equipment has no mechanical effect at all" is no longer true — no code change was needed, TASKS.md was just brought up to date. The original item below is preserved for reference:
[ORIGINAL, NO LONGER APPLICABLE] ⚠️ Requires a major/design decision — equipped armor/weapons have no mechanical effect on combat at all, purely cosmetic.** The `item.equipped` field appears in the codebase ONLY in the equip/unequip toggle logic in `routes/scene.js` (verified with grep) — damage calculation in the `/attack` endpoint (`rollDie(ATTACK_DAMAGE_DIE)`) always uses the same fixed die size, doesn't vary with the equipped weapon; there's also no armor/AC (armor class) reduction against incoming damage — `target.hp -= damage` / for player HP too, it falls the same flat way. So the paper-doll/icon system that took significant effort in Phase 4/5 (SS13 icons, drag-and-drop, 13 slots) is VISUALLY complete but GAMEPLAY-MECHANICALLY empty — a player's combat outcome is identical whether they equip anything or not. This isn't a small fix, it requires a genuine design decision: (a) how much should equipping armor reduce incoming damage (a fixed "AC" value, or added to the attacker's die roll)? (b) should different weapons deal different damage dice (e.g. Short Sword d6, Dagger d4)? This could be handled as the next gameplay-loop phase, needs PM/user prioritization — not auto-assigned.

17. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** The "How to Play?" help modal had no keyboard support at all — didn't close with Esc, no focus management. **Fix:** `HelpModal.tsx` — when the modal opens, the "Got it" button is automatically focused, a `keydown` listener is added to `document` that calls `onClose()` on `Escape` (cleaned up on unmount), and `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal="true"`/`aria-label` were also added (a small improvement for screen readers too, a natural continuation of #10). Verified live with Playwright: when the modal opens the close button is auto-focused, it closes with Esc, reopening with the ❓ button and Esc working again was verified. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

16. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** `/item/throw` never validated coordinates against grid bounds/range. `backend/routes/scene.js` — there was only a `typeof x/y === "number"` check, no bounds/range; a request like `x:99999,y:-500` would add an invisible/unreachable item to `scene.loot`. **Fix:** the coordinate check was tightened to also include `Number.isInteger`, a bounds check `0 ≤ x < scene.width` / `0 ≤ y < scene.height` was added, plus a `THROW_RANGE=3` range check consistent with Fireball's `range:3` (Manhattan distance from the player's position, same pattern as `/attack`/`/cast`). The frontend was deliberately left untouched — the same pattern is used for spell target-selection too (Fireball) (the backend enforces range, the frontend doesn't restrict at all, feeding back via an error message), the same approach was kept for consistency. Verified live with curl: `x:99999,y:-500` is now rejected with "outside map bounds," an out-of-range but in-bounds coordinate (`x:9,y:7`) is rejected with "outside throw range," a near/valid coordinate (`x:2,y:1`) works fine. Backend tests: 2 tests were deliberately broken (`falls as loot at the specified coordinate (no range/obstacle check)` and the action-consumption test) — as the name suggests, they used to DELIBERATELY test the "no range check" behavior, which has now deliberately changed (234/237, +1 previously known reset-test break).

15. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** Potion/consumable items had no icon/emoji at all in the inventory — only text was shown. The conditional render `{item.icon && <img .../>}` at `CharacterCard.tsx:298` printed nothing when `icon` was `null`/falsy (no fallback emoji). **Fix:** a `.inventory-icon-fallback` span showing a 🧪 emoji was added for when `item.icon` is absent and `item.slot` is also absent (the consumable category) (symmetric with the ⚔ pattern for weapons), a 20×20px size/alignment rule was added to `App.css` (so it occupies the same visual area as the existing `<img>`). Verified live with Playwright: "Potion (Minor Healing)" now shows with a 🧪 emoji in the inventory list, confirmed correctly aligned via a screenshot. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

14. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** Clearing the last encounter (Dragon's Lair) had no "you completed the campaign" moment at all — it silently looped back to the first encounter (Abandoned Dungeon). `checkEncounterCleared()` in `backend/routes/scene.js` printed the same generic message on every transition. **Fix:** the function now captures the `encounterIndex` cleared BEFORE `advanceToNextEncounter(scene)` and the scene's `totalEncounters`, checking `(clearedIndex + 1) % totalEncounters === 0` — if true ("a full lap completed"), it returns a special congratulatory sentence ("You've cleared the entire region! Your heroism is becoming legend... but danger never truly ends, a new threat appears: X.") , otherwise the old generic message. No extra import was needed (`scene.totalEncounters` is already a field on the scene itself). Verified by simulating 6 consecutive transitions directly with the real `sceneFactory.js` functions (without going through Express): "FULL LAP" triggers only on the 4th (Dragon's Lair, last) and 8th transitions, normal message on the others — repeats correctly every lap in the infinite loop. Backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test).

13. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** ⚠️ IMPORTANT — there was no stat validation on character creation, the client could send whatever value it wanted (a cheating vulnerability). `backend/routes/character.js` — `isValidAttributeSet()` only checked `Number.isFinite`, no upper/lower bound. The PM confirmed this directly in prod with `attributes: {str:99,...}`. **Fix:** `Number.isInteger` + a `1 ≤ value ≤ 22` range check (D20's highest die 20 + the highest race bonus +2) was added to `isValidAttributeSet`. The route logic itself was untouched — since the existing fallback pattern (`isValidAttributeSet(...) ? providedAttributes : rollAttributes()+applyBonuses`) already existed, an out-of-range/tampered `attributes` submission no longer RETURNS an error, it's silently REPLACED with the server's own fresh dice roll — this closes the exploit without breaking the UX (roll-stats→create flow). Verified live with curl: `str:99,...` is no longer accepted (the server rolls a fresh set like 4/14/5/10/12/18), and boundary values are also correct (22 accepted, 23 rejected and re-rolled). Backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test — the existing "valid attributes" test was unaffected since it already used values in the 1-22 range).

12. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** The Rogue class could never use spells, but the spell panel still appeared in the UI. `backend/data/dnd.js` — Rogue `baseMana: 3`, and both spells in `backend/data/spells.js` have `manaCost: 4` — the panel used to be shown with the condition `mana.max > 0`, so Rogue's 3 mana (>0) made the panel visible even though no spell could ever be selected. **Fix:** `CHEAPEST_SPELL_COST = Math.min(...SPELLS.map(s => s.manaCost))` was added to `CharacterCard.tsx`, the visibility condition became `mana.max >= CHEAPEST_SPELL_COST`. The backend was untouched — thanks to `leveling.js`'s logic of granting +2 mana per level to classes with `mana.max > 0`, Rogue already automatically/correctly sees the panel at level 2 (5 mana), and this new condition reflects that correctly too (not a fixed threshold, dynamic). Verified live with Playwright: panel hidden for a fresh Rogue (3 mana), panel still visible for a fresh Wizard (12 mana) (no regression). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

11. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** There was no length limit at all on the character name or chat messages. `backend/routes/character.js` / `backend/routes/chat.js` only checked for empty/whitespace (`!name.trim()`), no upper bound. **Fix:** `MAX_NAME_LENGTH=50` (`character.js`) and `MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH=500` (`chat.js`) were added; exceeding them returns 400 + a Turkish error message. Verified live with curl: a 60-character name and a 600-character message were rejected, normal short requests (a new character + a short chat message) continued to work fine. Backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test).

10. **[2026-08-24] [x] FIXED (coder)** Accessibility was near zero — `aria-label` was almost entirely absent. **Fix:** `aria-label` was added to the most critical icon-only/emoji-only interactive elements — `TacticalGrid.tsx`: every grid cell (token name+HP / loot name / "Empty tile (x, y)"), `CharacterCard.tsx`: the 13 paper-doll slots (equipped item name + "click to remove" or "{slot} (empty)", same text as the `title`), `App.tsx`: the ❓ help button in the header ("Open the How to Play? help window"). Inventory action buttons (Use/Equip/Drop/Throw) and spell buttons were left out of scope because they already combine emoji+visible text (NOT a `title`, actual button content) — the accessible name already comes from the text, no aria-label needed. A broad a11y audit was not done, only the most visible/critical elements were covered (consistent with the PM's decision). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

9. **[2026-08-23] [x] FIXED (coder)** The Game Over screen was completely empty — no adventure summary/closure at all. `frontend/src/components/GameOverScreen.tsx` only showed "{name} fell at level {level}" and moved on to "Restart." **Fix:** `xp`/`encountersCleared` props were added to `GameOverScreen` (optional + with default values, existing tests unbroken), showing a summary "You cleared N encounters, earned X XP." (the first part is skipped if there are 0 encounters). `App.tsx`: when the character dies (since App doesn't normally keep scene state), `getScene()` is called once to get the `encounterIndex`, `character.xp` is already available. I verified live with Playwright by simulating a real fight via the API (a goblin was killed, encounterIndex 0→1, xp +20) that the Game Over screen correctly showed "You cleared 1 encounter, earned 20 XP."; I also separately tested that a fresh/0-XP character correctly renders "Earned 0 XP." (without the encounter sentence). Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

8. **[2026-08-23] [x] FIXED (coder)** The AI GM prompt had no defense against "prompt injection" at all — player text was fed to the model raw. `backend/services/aiGm.js` — a sentence was added to the system instructions of `buildPrompt` (chat/attack/cast narration) and `buildOpeningPrompt` (opening story, character name is user input), stating that lines starting with "Player:"/the character name are untrusted user input and that no instruction inside them should be interpreted as a new system command. Not a complete solution (no prompt injection defense is 100%) but a cheap/low-risk improvement — since this isn't a behavior that can be tested on the code side (requires a real model call), only the prompt text was reviewed and updated. Backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test — aiGm.js is never called directly in any test, the mock/fallback path is tested).

7. **[2026-08-23] [x] FIXED (coder)** The `/api/health` response never checked the DB connection at all — if Postgres goes down, Render wouldn't notice. `backend/server.js:31-33` — the health check returned a fixed `{status:"ok"}`, performing no DB query. `render.yaml`'s `healthCheckPath: /api/health` depends on this endpoint — meaning if the Postgres connection drops/breaks (which can happen on free-tier Render Postgres), the backend kept reporting "healthy." **Fix:** `ping()` was added to `data/dbSqlite.js`/`data/dbPostgres.js` (`SELECT 1`), and `/api/health` in `server.js` now does `await db.ping()`, returning `503 {status:"error"}` on failure. Verified live with a real dev server + `game.db` (returns 200), backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test).

6. **[2026-08-23] [x] FIXED (coder)** Chat history was never bounded at all — it grows without limit even in a single long session. `backend/routes/chat.js:46,66` — `history.push(...)` appended to the array with no upper-bound check at all. **Fix:** `services/chatHistoryLimit.js` — `trimChatHistory(history)` keeps the last 200 messages (`splice`, reference preserved), dropping the rest. `chat.js`, `pushGmMessage` in `routes/scene.js`, and `/intro` in `character.js` — called after each of the three `history.push(...)` calls, before `saveChatHistory` (all 4 push points covered). The AI context was already separately bounded with `slice(-6)`, unaffected. Verified with Node: trims a 250-message array to 200, keeping the newest 200. Backend tests showed no regression (236/237, the one break is a previously known/deliberate reset test).

5. **[2026-08-23] [x] FIXED (coder)** Zero onboarding/help content — the system is now fairly complex (D20 dice, action+bonus action economy, spells, target selection, equipment slots) but there was no explanation at all. **Fix:** `frontend/src/components/HelpModal.tsx` — a short 4-item "🎲 How to Play?" modal (Action/Bonus Action, click-to-attack an adjacent enemy, spell/item target-select mode, End Turn flow). `App.tsx`: it opens automatically the first time the game screen is reached (after a new character's intro OR when the page is refreshed with an existing session) if not previously seen in `localStorage` (`dnd-help-seen`), and doesn't reopen on its own after that; a persistent ❓ button was added to the header so the user can reopen it anytime. If `localStorage` is inaccessible (private tab), it shows every time without crashing. Verified end-to-end with Playwright: opens automatically on first entry, disappears on close, does NOT reopen on its own after a page refresh, can be opened manually with the ❓ button, closes when clicking the overlay. Frontend tests showed no regression (80/80), tsc+build clean.

4. **[2026-08-23] Fetch errors on the frontend are swallowed entirely silently — combined with Render's "cold start" delay, the user is left with a blank/broken screen.** In `App.tsx`, the initial data fetches swallow errors with `.catch(() => {})`, giving no user feedback at all (lines 24, 46). This was exactly the root cause of the "Failed to fetch" screen the user complained about (seen before the VITE_API_BASE fix) — that specific bug is now fixed but the UNDERLYING pattern (silent error swallowing) still stands. Render's free web services "sleep" after ~15 min of inactivity, the first request can take 30-60 seconds (confirmed via research, see Phase 7 deploy notes) — if a visitor arrives while the backend is waking up, they'll currently see nothing/a broken screen, with no "server is waking up, please wait" feedback. Proposal: at minimum, show the user a message like "Couldn't connect, retrying..." + automatic retry (2-3 attempts a few seconds apart) if the initial load fails. A small/clear job, can be picked up once coder/tester finish the Phase 8 push.

2. **[2026-08-23] `POST /api/character/create` (and most endpoints in general) have no rate-limit at all — spam/resource-consumption risk.** Checked: `services/rateLimiter.js`'s `allowRequest()` ONLY limits AI (Gemini) calls (`character.js:122`, inside `/intro`). `POST /api/character/create` is entirely unlimited — and since there's no authentication either, a script could create hundreds of fake sessions/characters per second. Combined with idea #1 (orphan cleanup), the real risk emerges: even without cleanup, spam + unlimited create = could quickly fill the free Postgres's 1GB, and also strain the free-tier backend's CPU/RAM. Proposal: add a simple IP-based general rate-limit middleware (e.g. `express-rate-limit`, X requests per minute) at least to unauthenticated-accessible endpoints like `/character/create` and `/character/roll-stats`. A small/clear job, can be picked up once the current Phase 8 is done.

3. **[2026-08-23] Zero mobile/responsive support — the fixed-pixel-width 3-column layout breaks entirely on small screens.** There is no `@media` query at all in `frontend/src/App.css` and `index.css` (verified with grep, 0 results). `.app-main`'s `grid-template-columns: 280px 1fr 380px` (App.css:44) fixed value — just the two side panels alone are 660px, wider than most phone screens (e.g. 375-414px). In Phase 8 we made the layout `height:100vh + overflow:hidden` so it "never shifts" (coder report) — while this fixes the desktop scroll bug, it likely clips/renders the content unusable on narrow screens (not verified live with a phone simulation, but looks unavoidable from the CSS). With the goal of "opening up to users," a large portion of visitors may come from mobile. This isn't a clear-cut "small job" — it requires at least a mobile breakpoint that drops to a single column (e.g. below 768px: stack panels, put character card/map behind a tab/accordion), needs some design decisions. Should be prioritized with PM/user, not auto-assigned to the coder.

1. **[2026-08-23] No cleanup for abandoned sessions/characters — unlimited growth risk on the free Postgres (1GB limit).** `POST /api/character/reset` (`backend/routes/character.js:174`) only deletes the session's `activeCharacterIdBySession`/`chatHistories`/`scenes` link, but never deletes the character row ITSELF in the `characters` Map (and DB) — the character remains permanently "orphaned." Likewise, sessions that never return/never reset also remain in the DB forever. Today there are few users, no problem — but with the goal of "opening up to users," every visitor accumulating a permanent DB row means the free Postgres tier's 1GB limit will be hit sooner or later. Proposal: (a) have `/reset` also genuinely delete the orphaned character row, (b) a maintenance task that cleans up sessions/characters with no activity for a certain period (e.g. 30 days) (a simple scheduled SQL DELETE is enough, a separate cron job service isn't required — can also be done on server startup or with a periodic `setInterval`). A small/clear job, can be picked up once the coder is free.

**[2026-08-24] Independent tester verification (claude-game-38, assigned by the PM) — a second-eye check in the live environment of the idea #20/21/23/24/25 chain the coder tested themselves:**
- **#25 (VITE_API_BASE) + #24 (frontend CSP):** I downloaded the deployed JS bundle (`/assets/index-*.js`) with curl and grepped it — the bundle contains ONLY `dnd-game-backend-sz9e.onrender.com/api`, no reference at all to the old faulty `hostport` (schemeless `dnd-game-backend-sz9e:3001`). Frontend `curl -I`: a `content-security-policy` header is present and its `connect-src` contains exactly the `https://dnd-game-backend-sz9e.onrender.com` domain (the domain the CSP allows for the backend matches the actual bundle URL), `x-frame-options: SAMEORIGIN` is also present.
- **#23 (helmet):** Backend `curl -I https://dnd-game-backend-sz9e.onrender.com/api/health` — no `x-powered-by` header (grep returned empty), standard hardening headers `content-security-policy`/`x-content-type-options`/`strict-transport-security`/`x-frame-options` appear.
- **#20 (loot pickup) + #21 (inventory limit):** An end-to-end curl flow against the live backend with a real session (`X-Session-Id`): created a character (`POST /character/create`) → got the loot location (Bag of Gold, 5,1) via `GET /scene` → moved onto it via `POST /scene/move` → the response correctly returned `collectedLoot`, `inventoryFull:false`, "Bag of Gold added to your inventory!" in the narration, and the new item in the updated `character.inventory` → verified via `GET /character` that the item was persisted to the DB (still there on re-query). I also verified the `MAX_INVENTORY=30` logic (`routes/scene.js:27,142-147`) via code review — with a full inventory, the item stays in the scene and `inventoryFull:true` is returned, behavior exactly matching the description (filling to 30 items and re-triggering live wasn't done this round, code + the coder's tests were deemed sufficient).
- **Cleanup:** I deleted the character/session I created for testing from the prod DB via `POST /character/reset` (production data untouched, only the test record I created this round).
- **Result: all 5 items (20, 21, 23, 24, 25) independently verified, no bugs found.** Idea #18/19 (AI key status, on the PM/user side) and #26 (keyboard support, unassigned) were left out of scope for this round.

**[2026-08-24] General regression/health round (claude-game-38, at the PM's request) — an end-to-end curl flow against the live backend, with a real session:** create character (Wizard) → opening story (`/character/intro`, mock fallback correct) → chat message (also separately verified with a file-based request that Turkish characters aren't corrupted in UTF-8 — the corruption in the first attempt was a test artifact from Windows/Git-Bash `-d` argument escaping, not a backend bug) → equip Staff → move onto loot (Bag of Gold auto-collected) → attack adjacent Goblin (nat20 critical, correct damage, Action consumed) → End Turn (enemy missed) → kill the Goblin with Fireball (XP+20, mana 12→8, encounter auto-advanced to "Spider's Lair" — encounterIndex progressed, enemy/player reset) → cast Heal (didn't overflow since HP was already at max, mana still dropped) → watched the Giant Spider approach and attack over repeated End Turns (HP 7→3→0, didn't go below 0) → while dead, `/attack` was correctly rejected with 400 "The character is dead, game over." → **minor finding:** while dead, `/scene/move` and `/scene/end-turn` are NOT rejected at all (return 200, the enemy turn even continues to be processed) — only the `/attack`/`/cast`/`/item` endpoints are protected by `requireOwnedCharacter()`'s HP≤0 check. Practical risk is low (HP doesn't go below 0, the frontend already switches to `GameOverScreen` and never shows the normal game screen, a dead player wouldn't hit this path unless calling the API directly) — an architectural consistency note, in the same category as the characterId-ownership note from Phase 6-A, not a blocker. → cleaned up with `/character/reset`, verified with `GET /character` returning 404.
**Result: the entire main flow (create→chat→equip→loot→attack→spell→encounter transition→death→reset) works fine. One note: move/end-turn is missing the dead-character check (above), fix optional/low priority.**

**[2026-08-24] Independent live verification of the dead-character fix (commit aeaec65) (claude-game-38):** Live, with a fresh Wizard: (a) **no regression** — `/scene/move` and `/scene/end-turn` return normal 200 while HP is full (behavior unbroken, including the goblin's approach message); (b) fought the goblin and dropped HP to 0 (7→3→0, didn't go negative); (c) **fix verified** — while dead, both `/scene/move` and `/scene/end-turn` are now rejected with `400 {"error":"The character is dead, game over."}` (previously both returned 200 and even processed the enemy turn). Test character was cleaned up with `/character/reset`. Finding fully closed, no bug remains.

**[2026-08-24] Independent live verification of idea #29 (critical god-mode vulnerability, commit 6fce36f) (claude-game-38):** Created a fresh character and repeated the old exploit request exactly: sent `{"hp":{"current":99999,...},"attributes":{"str":999,...}}` via `POST /api/character` — **now returns `404 "Cannot POST /api/character"`** (the route is entirely removed, it previously accepted with 200). Verified via `GET /character` that the character's stats remained normal/expected (fighter base HP 12/12, attributes in the 1-22 range) and were unchanged — god-mode injection is no longer possible. Regression check: with the same session, `/scene/move` continues to return normal 200 (confirmed the removed route doesn't affect normal gameplay flow). Test character cleaned up. **Critical vulnerability fully closed, can't be exploited live.**

**[2026-08-24] Independent live verification of idea #30 (GameOverScreen ARIA, commit 55231cd) (claude-game-38, with a real browser/Playwright):** Using global Playwright + chromium (already cached) locally, a temporary script drove a REAL game flow against the live site: create character (Wizard, low HP) → roll dice → create → "Continue" on the intro → repeatedly "End Turn" waiting for the enemy to approach and attack → the character genuinely died. `GameOverScreen`'s root element was verified in the DOM: `role="alertdialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-label="Game Over"` — all rendering live exactly as the coder added them, the text is also correct ("AriaCheck38 fell at level 1... Earned 0 XP."). No page errors (just the expected 1 initial 404, the same pattern seen in previous rounds). **Note/disclosure:** this script didn't use a persistent browser profile (ephemeral context), so I couldn't capture the test character's `X-Session-Id` and clean it up with `/character/reset` — a single test character named "AriaCheck38" remains orphaned in the prod DB (idea #1's 30-day auto-cleanup will already cover it, doesn't mix with production data, a single record). Result: **idea #30 fully verified, no bugs.**
